> SYSTEM

Usage: /loop [interval] <prompt>

Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval.

Intervals: Ns, Nm, Nh, Nd (e.g. 5m, 30m, 2h, 1d). Minimum granularity is 1 minute.
If no interval is specified, defaults to 10m.

Examples:
  /loop 5m /babysit-prs
  /loop 30m check the deploy
  /loop 1h /standup 1
  /loop check the deploy          (defaults to 10m)
  /loop check the deploy every 20m

- `/loop 30m check the deploy status`
- `/loop 1h run the test suite`

> SYSTEM

# /loop — schedule a recurring prompt

Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it with CronCreate.

## Parsing (in priority order)

1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt.
2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval.
3. **Default**: otherwise, interval is `10m` and the entire input is the prompt.

If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop — do not call CronCreate.

Examples:
- `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1)
- `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2)
- `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2)
- `check the deploy` → interval `10m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3)
- `check every PR` → interval `10m`, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time)
- `5m` → empty prompt → show usage

## Interval → cron

Supported suffixes: `s` (seconds, rounded up to nearest minute, min 1), `m` (minutes), `h` (hours), `d` (days). Convert:

| Interval pattern      | Cron expression     | Notes                                    |
|-----------------------|---------------------|------------------------------------------|
| `Nm` where N ≤ 59   | `*/N * * * *`     | every N minutes                          |
| `Nm` where N ≥ 60   | `0 */H * * *`     | round to hours (H = N/60, must divide 24)|
| `Nh` where N ≤ 23   | `0 */N * * *`     | every N hours                            |
| `Nd`                | `0 0 */N * *`     | every N days at midnight local           |
| `Ns`                | treat as `ceil(N/60)m` | cron minimum granularity is 1 minute  |

**If the interval doesn't cleanly divide its unit** (e.g. `7m` → `*/7 * * * *` gives uneven gaps at :56→:00; `90m` → 1.5h which cron can't express), pick the nearest clean interval and tell the user what you rounded to before scheduling.

## Action

Call CronCreate with:
- `cron`: the expression from the table above
- `prompt`: the parsed prompt from above, verbatim (slash commands are passed through unchanged)
- `recurring`: `true`

Then confirm to the user: what's scheduled, the cron expression, the human-readable cadence, that recurring tasks auto-expire after 3 days, and that they can cancel sooner with CronDelete (include the job ID).

-import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
-import { writeClipboardTextSafe } from '../clipboard';
-
-describe('writeClipboardTextSafe', () => {
-  const originalClipboard = navigator.clipboard;
-  const originalExecCommand = document.execCommand;
-
-  afterEach(() => {
-    Object.assign(navigator, { clipboard: originalClipboard });
-    if (originalExecCommand) {
-      Object.assign(document, { execCommand: originalExecCommand });
-    } else {
-      delete (document as Document & { execCommand?: typeof document.execCommand }).execCommand;
-    }
-    vi.restoreAllMocks();
-  });
-
-  it('returns false for empty text', async () => {
-    await expect(writeClipboardTextSafe('')).resolves.toBe(false);
-  });
-
-  it('returns true when navigator clipboard write succeeds', async () => {
-    const writeText = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined);
-    Object.assign(navigator, {
-      clipboard: { writeText },
-    });
-
-    await expect(writeClipboardTextSafe('hello')).resolves.toBe(true);
-    expect(writeText).toHaveBeenCalledWith('hello');
-  });
-
-  it('returns false when fallback copy command reports failure', async () => {
-    Object.assign(navigator, {
-      clipboard: {
-        writeText: vi.fn().mockRejectedValue(new Error('no clipboard permission')),
-      },
-    });
-    Object.assign(document, {
-      execCommand: vi.fn().mockReturnValue(false),
-    });
-
-    await expect(
-      writeClipboardTextSafe('hello', { allowExecCommandFallback: true }),
-    ).resolves.toBe(false);
-  });
-
-  it('returns true when fallback copy command succeeds', async () => {
-    Object.assign(navigator, {
-      clipboard: {
-        writeText: vi.fn().mockRejectedValue(new Error('no clipboard permission')),
-      },
-    });
-    Object.assign(document, {
-      execCommand: vi.fn().mockReturnValue(true),
-    });
-
-    await expect(
-      writeClipboardTextSafe('hello', { allowExecCommandFallback: true }),
-    ).resolves.toBe(true);
-  });
-});

diff --git a/src/shared/lib/clipboard.ts b/src/shared/lib/clipboard.ts
deleted file mode 100644
index 7c8e88cff..000000000
--- a/src/shared/lib/clipboard.ts
+++ /dev/null
@@ -1,42 +0,0 @@
-/**
- * Best-effort clipboard write that never throws.
- * Returns true when the write succeeds.
- */
-export async function writeClipboardTextSafe(
-  text: string,
-  options: { allowExecCommandFallback?: boolean } = {},
-): Promise<boolean> {
-  if (!text) {
-    return false;
-  }
-
-  if (typeof navigator !== 'undefined' && navigator.clipboard?.writeText) {
-    try {
-      await navigator.clipboard.writeText(text);
-      return true;
-    } catch {
-      // fall through to optional fallback
-    }
-  }
-
-  if (!options.allowExecCommandFallback || typeof document === 'undefined') {
-    return false;
-  }
-
-  let textarea: HTMLTextAreaElement | null = null;
-  try {
-    textarea = document.createElement('textarea');
-    textarea.value = text;
-    textarea.style.position = 'fixed';
-    textarea.style.left = '-9999px';
-    document.body.appendChild(textarea);
-    textarea.select();
-    return document.execCommand('copy') === true;
-  } catch {
-    return false;
-  } finally {
-    if (textarea?.parentNode) {
-      textarea.parentNode.removeChild(textarea);
-    }
-  }
-}
diff --git a/src/shared/lib/fileConversion.ts b/src/shared/lib/fileConversion.ts
deleted file mode 100644
index c2230717e..000000000
--- a/src/shared/lib/fileConversion.ts
+++ /dev/null
@@ -1,36 +0,0 @@
-export const fileToDataURL = (file: File): Promise<string> =>
-  new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
-    const reader = new FileReader();
-    reader.onload = () => resolve(reader.result as string);
-    reader.onerror = (error) => reject(error);
-    reader.readAsDataURL(file);
-  });
-
-export const dataURLtoFile = (
-  dataUrl: string,
-  filename: string,
-  fileType?: string
-): File | null => {
-  try {
-    const [metadata, payload] = dataUrl.split(',');
-    if (!metadata || !payload) {
-      throw new Error('Invalid Data URL format');
-    }
-
-    const mimeMatch = metadata.match(/:(.*?);/);
-    const mimeType =
-      fileType ||
-      (mimeMatch && mimeMatch[1]) ||
-      'application/octet-stream';
-    const decodedPayload = atob(payload);
-    const bytes = new Uint8Array(decodedPayload.length);
-
-    for (let i = 0; i < decodedPayload.length; i += 1) {
-      bytes[i] = decodedPayload.charCodeAt(i);
-    }
-
-    return new File([bytes], filename, { type: mimeType });
-  } catch {
-    return null;
-  }
-};
diff --git a/src/shared/lib/generationTaskIdParser.ts b/src/shared/lib/generationTaskIdParser.ts
deleted file mode 100644

--- a/src/shared/lib/generationTaskIdParser.ts
+++ /dev/null
@@ -1,45 +0,0 @@
-type GenerationTaskIdParseStatus = 'ok' | 'invalid_tasks_shape';
-
-interface GenerationTaskIdParseResult {
-  taskId: string | null;
-  status: GenerationTaskIdParseStatus;
-}
-
-function normalizeCandidateTaskId(taskId: string): string | null {
-  const normalized = taskId.trim();
-  return normalized.length > 0 ? normalized : null;
-}
-
-/**
- * Parse `generations.tasks` into a canonical primary task ID representation.
- * Supports legacy `string` and canonical `string[]` storage shapes.
- */
-export function parseGenerationTaskId(tasks: unknown): GenerationTaskIdParseResult {
-  if (tasks === null || tasks === undefined) {
-    return { taskId: null, status: 'ok' };
-  }
-
-  if (typeof tasks === 'string') {
-    const parsedTaskId = normalizeCandidateTaskId(tasks);
-    return parsedTaskId
-      ? { taskId: parsedTaskId, status: 'ok' }
-      : { taskId: null, status: 'invalid_tasks_shape' };
-  }
-
-  if (!Array.isArray(tasks)) {
-    return { taskId: null, status: 'invalid_tasks_shape' };
-  }
-
-  if (!tasks.every((item) => typeof item === 'string')) {
-    return { taskId: null, status: 'invalid_tasks_shape' };
-  }
-
-  if (tasks.length === 0) {
-    return { taskId: null, status: 'ok' };
-  }
-
-  const parsedTaskId = normalizeCandidateTaskId(tasks[0]);
-  return parsedTaskId
-    ? { taskId: parsedTaskId, status: 'ok' }
-    : { taskId: null, status: 'invalid_tasks_shape' };
-}

   574→+function isOrchestratorTask(taskType: string | null | undefined): boolean {
   575→+  return taskType?.toLowerCase().includes('orchestrator') ?? false;
   576→+}
   577→+
   578→+function hasPositiveCredits(value: unknown): boolean {
   579→+  if (typeof value === 'number') {
   580→+    return Number.isFinite(value) && value > 0;
   581→+  }
   582→+  if (typeof value === 'string') {
   583→+    const parsed = Number(value);
   584→+    return Number.isFinite(parsed) && parsed > 0;
   585→+  }
   586→+  return false;
   587→+}
   588→+
   589→+function collectDependencyIds(tasks: Array<{ dependant_on?: string[] | null }>): string[] {
   590→+  const dependencyIds = new Set<string>();
   591→+  for (const task of tasks) {
   592→+    if (!Array.isArray(task.dependant_on)) {
   593→+      continue;
   594→+    }
   595→+    for (const dependencyId of task.dependant_on) {
   596→+      dependencyIds.add(dependencyId);
   597→+    }
   598→+  }
   599→+  return Array.from(dependencyIds);
   600→+}
   601→+
   602→+function allDependenciesComplete(
   603→+  dependantOn: string[] | null | undefined,
   604→+  completedDependencyIds: Set<string>,
   605→+): boolean {
   606→+  if (!dependantOn || dependantOn.length === 0) {
   607→+    return true;
   608→+  }
   609→+  return dependantOn.every((dependencyId) => completedDependencyIds.has(dependencyId));
   610→+}

1→import { useState, useEffect, useRef, useCallback, useMemo } from 'react';
     2→import { getSupabaseClient as supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';
     3→import { useRenderLogger } from '@/shared/lib/debug/debugRendering';

     5→import { updateToolSettingsSupabase } from '@/shared/hooks/settings/useToolSettings';
     6→import { normalizeAndPresentError } from '@/shared/lib/errorHandling/runtimeError';
     7→import { SETTINGS_IDS } from '@/shared/lib/settingsIds';
     8→
     9→// Module-level request deduplication cache for the raw `users.settings` DB fetch.
    10→//
    11→// WHY THIS EXISTS (instead of React Query):
    12→// This hook reads a single key from `users.settings.ui`, but many instances
    13→// mount concurrently for different keys (generationMethods, privacyDefaults,
    14→// theme, etc.). Without deduplication, each mount fires an identical
    15→// `SELECT settings FROM users WHERE id = ?` query.
    16→//
    17→// WHY NOT React Query: The hook's read path has complex per-key logic
    18→// (fallback merging, normalization, auto-backfill of missing DB fields)
    19→// that runs after fetching the raw settings row. Migrating to React Query
    20→// would require either (a) a single shared query that all instances subscribe
    21→// to (losing per-key granularity for loading/error states), or (b) per-key
    22→// queries that each fetch the same row (defeating deduplication). The current
    23→// approach deduplicates at the network layer while keeping per-key logic local.
    24→//
    25→// WRITES already go through useToolSettings' global write queue, so there is
    26→// no write-side duplication -- only this read-side cache is parallel.
    27→interface CachedUserSettingsRow {
    28→  settings?: Json;
    29→}
    30→interface CachedSettingsResult {
    31→  data: CachedUserSettingsRow | null;
    32→  error: unknown;
    33→}
    34→interface SettingsCacheEntry {
    35→  data: CachedSettingsResult | null;
    36→  timestamp: number;
    37→  loading: Promise<CachedSettingsResult> | null;
    38→}
    39→const settingsCache = new Map<string, SettingsCacheEntry>();
    40→const CACHE_DURATION = 30000; // 30 seconds
    41→
    42→interface UISettings {
    43→  paneLocks: {
    44→    shots: boolean;
    45→    tasks: boolean;
    46→    gens: boolean;
    47→  };
    48→  settingsModal: {
    49→    activeTab: string;
    50→  };
    51→  videoTravelWidescreen: {
    52→    enabled: boolean;
    53→  };
    54→  imageDeletion?: {
    55→    skipConfirmation: boolean;
    56→  };
    57→  generationMethods: {
    58→    onComputer: boolean;
    59→    inCloud: boolean;
    60→  };
    61→  aiInputMode: {
    62→    mode: 'voice' | 'text';
    63→  };
    64→  privacyDefaults: {
    65→    resourcesPublic: boolean;
    66→    generationsPublic: boolean;
    67→  };
    68→  theme: {
    69→    darkMode: boolean; // Defaults to true (dark mode) for new users
    70→  };
    71→  productTour?: {
    72→    completed: boolean;
    73→    skipped: boolean;
    74→  };
    75→}
    76→
    77→function toRecord(value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> | undefined {
    78→  if (value && typeof value === 'object' && !Array.isArray(value)) {
    79→    return value as Record<string, unknown>;
    80→  }
    81→  return undefined;
    82→}
    83→
    84→function readBoolean(value: unknown, fallback: boolean): boolean {
    85→  return typeof value === 'boolean' ? value : fallback;
    86→}
    87→
    88→function readString(value: unknown, fallback: string): string {
    89→  return typeof value === 'string' ? value : fallback;
    90→}
    91→
    92→function decodePaneLocks(
    93→  value: unknown,
    94→  fallback: UISettings['paneLocks'],
    95→): UISettings['paneLocks'] {
    96→  const record = toRecord(value);
    97→  return {
    98→    shots: readBoolean(record?.shots, fallback.shots),
    99→    tasks: readBoolean(record?.tasks, fallback.tasks),
   100→    gens: readBoolean(record?.gens, fallback.gens),
   101→  };
   102→}
   103→
   104→function decodeSettingsModal(
   105→  value: unknown,
   106→  fallback: UISettings['settingsModal'],
   107→): UISettings['settingsModal'] {
   108→  const record = toRecord(value);
   109→  return {
   110→    activeTab: readString(record?.activeTab, fallback.activeTab),
   111→  };
   112→}
   113→
   114→function decodeVideoTravelWidescreen(
   115→  value: unknown,
   116→  fallback: UISettings['videoTravelWidescreen'],
   117→): UISettings['videoTravelWidescreen'] {
   118→  const record = toRecord(value);
   119→  return {
   120→    enabled: readBoolean(record?.enabled, fallback.enabled),
   121→  };
   122→}
   123→
   124→function decodeImageDeletion(
   125→  value: unknown,
   126→  fallback: NonNullable<UISettings['imageDeletion']>,
   127→): NonNullable<UISettings['imageDeletion']> {
   128→  const record = toRecord(value);
   129→  return {
   130→    skipConfirmation: readBoolean(record?.skipConfirmation, fallback.skipConfirmation),
   131→  };
   132→}
   133→
   134→function decodeGenerationMethods(
   135→  value: unknown,
   136→  fallback: UISettings['generationMethods'],
   137→): UISettings['generationMethods'] {
   138→  const record = toRecord(value);
   139→  const normalized = {
   140→    inCloud: readBoolean(record?.inCloud, fallback.inCloud),
   141→    onComputer: readBoolean(record?.onComputer, fallback.onComputer),
   142→  };
   143→
   144→  if (normalized.inCloud && normalized.onComputer) {
   145→    return {
   146→      inCloud: true,
   147→      onComputer: false,
   148→    };
   149→  }
   150→
   151→  return normalized;
   152→}
   153→
   154→function decodeAIInputMode(
   155→  value: unknown,
   156→  fallback: UISettings['aiInputMode'],
   157→): UISettings['aiInputMode'] {
   158→  const record = toRecord(value);
   159→  const mode = record?.mode === 'voice' || record?.mode === 'text'
   160→    ? record.mode
   161→    : fallback.mode;
   162→
   163→  return { mode };
   164→}
   165→
   166→function decodePrivacyDefaults(
   167→  value: unknown,
   168→  fallback: UISettings['privacyDefaults'],
   169→): UISettings['privacyDefaults'] {
   170→  const record = toRecord(value);
   171→  return {
   172→    resourcesPublic: readBoolean(record?.resourcesPublic, fallback.resourcesPublic),
   173→    generationsPublic: readBoolean(record?.generationsPublic, fallback.generationsPublic),
   174→  };
   175→}
   176→
   177→function decodeTheme(
   178→  value: unknown,
   179→  fallback: UISettings['theme'],
   180→): UISettings['theme'] {
   181→  const record = toRecord(value);
   182→  return {
   183→    darkMode: readBoolean(record?.darkMode, fallback.darkMode),
   184→  };
   185→}
   186→
   187→function decodeProductTour(
   188→  value: unknown,
   189→  fallback: NonNullable<UISettings['productTour']>,
   190→): NonNullable<UISettings['productTour']> {
   191→  const record = toRecord(value);
   192→  return {
   193→    completed: readBoolean(record?.completed, fallback.completed),
   194→    skipped: readBoolean(record?.skipped, fallback.skipped),
   195→  };
   196→}
   197→
   198→function normalizeUserUISetting<K extends keyof UISettings>(
   199→  key: K,
   200→  value: unknown,
   201→  fallback: UISettings[K],
   202→): UISettings[K] {
   203→  switch (key) {
   204→    case 'paneLocks':
   205→      return decodePaneLocks(value, fallback as UISettings['paneLocks']) as UISettings[K];
   206→    case 'settingsModal':
   207→      return decodeSettingsModal(value, fallback as UISettings['settingsModal']) as UISettings[K];
   208→    case 'videoTravelWidescreen':
   209→      return decodeVideoTravelWidescreen(
   210→        value,
   211→        fallback as UISettings['videoTravelWidescreen'],
   212→      ) as UISettings[K];
   213→    case 'imageDeletion':
   214→      return decodeImageDeletion(
   215→        value,
   216→        fallback as NonNullable<UISettings['imageDeletion']>,
   217→      ) as UISettings[K];
   218→    case 'generationMethods':
   219→      return decodeGenerationMethods(
   220→        value,
   221→        fallback as UISettings['generationMethods'],
   222→      ) as UISettings[K];
   223→    case 'aiInputMode':
   224→      return decodeAIInputMode(value, fallback as UISettings['aiInputMode']) as UISettings[K];
   225→    case 'privacyDefaults':
   226→      return decodePrivacyDefaults(
   227→        value,
   228→        fallback as UISettings['privacyDefaults'],
   229→      ) as UISettings[K];
   230→    case 'theme':
   231→      return decodeTheme(value, fallback as UISettings['theme']) as UISettings[K];
   232→    case 'productTour':
   233→      return decodeProductTour(
   234→        value,
   235→        fallback as NonNullable<UISettings['productTour']>,
   236→      ) as UISettings[K];
   237→    default:
   238→      return fallback;
   239→  }
   240→}
   241→
   242→function mergeSettingRecords(currentValue: unknown, patch: unknown): Record<string, unknown> {
   243→  return {
   244→    ...(toRecord(currentValue) ?? {}),
   245→    ...(toRecord(patch) ?? {}),
   246→  };
   247→}
   248→
   249→function hasPersistedValueDrift(value: unknown, normalizedValue: unknown): boolean {
   250→  return JSON.stringify(value) !== JSON.stringify(normalizedValue);
   251→}
   252→
   253→// Cached settings loader to prevent duplicate database calls  
   254→const loadUserSettingsCached = async (userId: string) => {
   255→  const cacheKey = `user_settings_${userId}`;
   256→  const cached = settingsCache.get(cacheKey);
   257→  
   258→  // Return fresh cached data
   259→  if (cached && (Date.now() - cached.timestamp) < CACHE_DURATION) {
   260→    return cached.data;
   261→  }
   262→  
   263→  // If there's already a loading request, wait for it
   264→  if (cached?.loading) {
   265→    return await cached.loading;
   266→  }
   267→  
   268→  // Make new database call and cache the promise
   269→  const loadingPromise: Promise<CachedSettingsResult> = (async () => {
   270→    try {
   271→      const result = await supabase().from('users')
   272→        .select('settings')
   273→        .eq('id', userId)
   274→        .single();
   275→      const typedResult: CachedSettingsResult = {
   276→        data: result.data ? { settings: result.data.settings } : null,
   277→        error: result.error,
   278→      };
   279→      settingsCache.set(cacheKey, {
   280→        data: typedResult,
   281→        timestamp: Date.now(),
   282→        loading: null
   283→      });
   284→      return typedResult;
   285→    } catch (error) {
   286→      settingsCache.delete(cacheKey);
   287→      throw error;
   288→    }
   289→  })();
   290→  
   291→  // Cache the loading promise to prevent duplicate requests
   292→  settingsCache.set(cacheKey, {
   293→    data: cached?.data || null,
   294→    timestamp: cached?.timestamp || 0,
   295→    loading: loadingPromise
   296→  });
   297→  
   298→  return await loadingPromise;
   299→};
   300→
   301→/**
   302→ * Hook for user-scoped UI preferences (theme, pane locks, generation methods, etc.).
   303→ *
   304→ * Scope: user-only (stored in `users.settings.ui`).
   305→ * Auto-save: yes, 200ms debounce via the global settings write queue.
   306→ *
   307→ * Use this for preferences that follow the user across all projects/shots.
   308→ * For project/shot-scoped settings, use `useAutoSaveSettings` instead.
   309→ *
   310→ * @see docs/structure_detail/settings_system.md for the full settings hook decision tree
   311→ */
   312→export function useUserUIState<K extends keyof UISettings>(
   313→  key: K,
   314→  fallback: UISettings[K]
   315→) {
   316→  // Stabilize fallback using a ref so callers can pass inline objects without causing
   317→  // infinite render loops. The load effect reads fallbackRef.current instead of depending
   318→  // on fallback directly, preventing re-runs when the reference changes but values don't.
   319→  const fallbackRef = useRef<UISettings[K]>(fallback);
   320→  fallbackRef.current = fallback;
   321→
   322→  useRenderLogger(`UserUIState:${key}`);
   323→
   324→  const [value, setValue] = useState<UISettings[K]>(fallback);
   325→  const [isLoading, setIsLoading] = useState(true);
   326→  const userIdRef = useRef<string>();
   327→  const debounceRef = useRef<NodeJS.Timeout>();
   328→  // Ref so that `update` can read the latest value without depending on it
   329→  const valueRef = useRef(value);
   330→  valueRef.current = value;
   331→
   332→  // Helper function to save fallback values to database (preserves all existing settings)
   333→  // This automatically backfills existing users with default values when they first load the app
   334→  // IMPORTANT: Only runs when the key is completely missing from database (undefined)
   335→  // Does NOT override explicit user choices (including both options set to false)
   336→  const saveFallbackToDatabase = useCallback(async (userId: string) => {
   337→    try {
   338→      const fallbackToSave = normalizeUserUISetting(key, fallbackRef.current, fallbackRef.current);
   339→
   340→      // Use the global queue to save - it handles merging
   341→      await updateToolSettingsSupabase({
   342→        scope: 'user',
   343→        id: userId,
   344→        toolId: SETTINGS_IDS.USER_UI_STATE,
   345→        patch: { [key]: fallbackToSave },
   346→      }, 'immediate');
   347→
   348→      // Invalidate cache so other components see the backfilled values
   349→      const cacheKey = `user_settings_${userId}`;
   350→      settingsCache.delete(cacheKey);
   351→      setValue(fallbackToSave); // Update local state after successful save
   352→    } catch (error) {
   353→      normalizeAndPresentError(error, { context: 'useUserUIState.saveFallbackToDatabase', showToast: false });
   354→    }
   355→  }, [key]);
   356→
   357→  // Load initial value from database
   358→  useEffect(() => {
   359→    const loadUserSettings = async () => {
   360→      try {
   361→        const { data: { user } } = await supabase().auth.getUser();
   362→        if (!user) {
   363→          // Skip loading for unauthenticated users (e.g., on public share pages)
   364→          setIsLoading(false);
   365→          return;
   366→        }
   367→
   368→        userIdRef.current = user.id;
   369→
   370→        const cachedResult = await loadUserSettingsCached(user.id);
   371→        const data = cachedResult?.data ?? null;
   372→        const error = cachedResult?.error ?? null;
   373→
   374→        if (error) {
   375→          normalizeAndPresentError(error, { context: 'useUserUIState.loadSettings', showToast: false });
   376→          setIsLoading(false);
   377→          return;
   378→        }
   379→
   380→        const settingsRecord = toRecord(data?.settings);
   381→        const uiSettings = toRecord(settingsRecord?.ui);
   382→        const keyValue = uiSettings?.[key];
   383→
   384→        if (keyValue !== undefined) {
   385→          const normalizedValue = normalizeUserUISetting(key, keyValue, fallbackRef.current);
   386→          setValue(normalizedValue);
   387→
   388→          // If any fields from the fallback are missing in the stored value OR
   389→          // normalization changed the value, backfill them in DB
   390→          if (hasPersistedValueDrift(keyValue, normalizedValue)) {
   391→            // Use the global queue to save - it handles merging
   392→            updateToolSettingsSupabase({
   393→              scope: 'user',
   394→              id: user.id,
   395→              toolId: SETTINGS_IDS.USER_UI_STATE,
   396→              patch: { [key]: normalizedValue },
   397→            }, 'immediate').then(() => {
   398→              const cacheKey = `user_settings_${user.id}`;
   399→              settingsCache.delete(cacheKey);
   400→            }).catch(e => {
   401→              normalizeAndPresentError(e, { context: 'useUserUIState.backfillFields', showToast: false });
   402→            });
   403→          }
   404→        } else {
   405→          // Key doesn't exist in database - this is an existing user who hasn't set preferences yet
   406→          // Save fallback values to backfill them (only runs when completely empty)
   407→          const normalizedFallback = normalizeUserUISetting(key, fallbackRef.current, fallbackRef.current);
   408→          setValue(normalizedFallback); // Set normalized fallback immediately for responsive UI
   409→
   410→          // Save to database in background (don't block loading)
   411→          saveFallbackToDatabase(user.id).catch(error => {
   412→            normalizeAndPresentError(error, { context: 'useUserUIState.saveFallback', showToast: false });
   413→          });
   414→        }
   415→
   416→        setIsLoading(false);
   417→      } catch (error) {
   418→        normalizeAndPresentError(error, { context: 'useUserUIState.loadUserSettings', showToast: false });
   419→        setIsLoading(false);
   420→      }
   421→    };
   422→
   423→    loadUserSettings();
   424→  }, [key, saveFallbackToDatabase]);
   425→
   426→  // Debounced update function
   427→  // Uses the global settings write queue (via updateToolSettingsSupabase) to prevent
   428→  // race conditions with other hooks that write to users.settings (e.g., useToolSettings
   429→  // writing user-preferences). The write queue serializes writes and uses an atomic RPC.
   430→  const update = useCallback((patch: Partial<UISettings[K]>) => {
   431→    // Immediately update local state for responsive UI (with normalization)
   432→    const normalizedPatch = normalizeUserUISetting(
   433→      key,
   434→      mergeSettingRecords(valueRef.current, patch),
   435→      fallbackRef.current,
   436→    );
   437→    setValue(normalizedPatch);
   438→
   439→    // Clear existing timeout
   440→    if (debounceRef.current) {
   441→      clearTimeout(debounceRef.current);
   442→    }
   443→
   444→    // Debounce the database write via the global settings write queue
   445→    debounceRef.current = setTimeout(async () => {
   446→      const userId = userIdRef.current;
   447→      if (!userId) return;
   448→
   449→      try {
   450→        // Use the write queue which handles atomic DB updates and merging.
   451→        // This prevents race conditions with other hooks writing to users.settings.
   452→        await updateToolSettingsSupabase({
   453→          scope: 'user',
   454→          id: userId,
   455→          toolId: SETTINGS_IDS.USER_UI_STATE,
   456→          patch: { [key]: normalizedPatch },
   457→        });
   458→
   459→        // Invalidate local cache so other useUserUIState instances see the update
   460→        const cacheKey = `user_settings_${userId}`;
   461→        settingsCache.delete(cacheKey);
   462→      } catch (error) {
   463→        normalizeAndPresentError(error, { context: 'useUserUIState.update', showToast: false });
   464→      }
   465→    }, 200);
   466→  }, [key]);
   467→
   468→  // Cleanup timeout on unmount
   469→  useEffect(() => {
   470→    return () => {
   471→      if (debounceRef.current) {
   472→        clearTimeout(debounceRef.current);
   473→      }
   474→    };
   475→  }, []);
   476→
   477→  return useMemo(() => ({
   478→    value,
   479→    update,
   480→    isLoading
   481→  }), [value, update, isLoading]);
   482→}
   483→
   484→/** @internal Only for test isolation — do not call in production code. */
   485→export function _resetUserUIStateCacheForTesting(): void {
   486→  settingsCache.clear();
   487→}
   488→

   126→interface ToolSettingsContext {
   127→  projectId?: string;
   128→  shotId?: string;

   130→type ToolSettingsErrorCode =
   131→  | 'auth_required'
   132→  | 'cancelled'
   133→  | 'network'
   134→  | 'scope_fetch_failed'
   135→  | 'invalid_scope_identifier'
   136→  | 'unknown';
   137→interface ToolSettingsErrorOptions {
   138→  recoverable?: boolean;
   139→  cause?: unknown;
   140→  metadata?: Record<string, unknown>;
   141→}
   142→export class ToolSettingsError extends Error {
   143→  readonly code: ToolSettingsErrorCode;
   144→  readonly recoverable: boolean;
   145→  readonly metadata?: Record<string, unknown>;
   146→  readonly cause?: unknown;
   147→  constructor(
   148→    code: ToolSettingsErrorCode,
   149→    message: string,
   150→    options: ToolSettingsErrorOptions = {},
   151→  ) {
   152→    super(message);
   153→    this.name = 'ToolSettingsError';
   154→    this.code = code;
   155→    this.recoverable = options.recoverable ?? false;
   156→    this.metadata = options.metadata;
   157→    this.cause = options.cause;
   158→  }
   159→}
   160→function isToolSettingsError(error: unknown): error is ToolSettingsError {
   161→  return error instanceof ToolSettingsError;
   162→}
   163→export function classifyToolSettingsError(error: unknown): ToolSettingsError {
   164→  if (isToolSettingsError(error)) {
   165→    return error;
   166→  }
   167→  if (isCancellationError(error)) {
   168→    return new ToolSettingsError('cancelled', 'Request was cancelled', {
   169→      recoverable: true,
   170→      cause: error,
   171→    });
   172→  }
   173→  const message = getErrorMessage(error);
   174→  if (message.includes('Authentication required')) {
   175→    return new ToolSettingsError('auth_required', message, {
   176→      recoverable: false,
   177→      cause: error,
   178→    });
   179→  }
   180→  if (
   181→    message.includes('Failed to fetch')
   182→    || message.includes('ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES')
   183→    || message.includes('Network connection issue')
   184→    || message.includes('Network exhaustion')
   185→  ) {
   186→    return new ToolSettingsError('network', message, {
   187→      recoverable: true,
   188→      cause: error,
   189→    });
   190→  }
   191→  return new ToolSettingsError('unknown', message, {
   192→    recoverable: false,
   193→    cause: error,
   194→  });
   195→}
   196→function toOperationFailure(error: ToolSettingsError): OperationFailure {
   197→  return operationFailure(error, {
   198→    policy: error.recoverable ? 'best_effort' : 'fail_closed',
   199→    recoverable: error.recoverable,
   200→    errorCode: error.code,
   201→    message: error.message,
   202→    cause: error.cause,
   203→  });
   204→}
   205→function toToolSettingsErrorFromOperationFailure(failure: OperationFailure): ToolSettingsError {
   206→  const code = failure.errorCode as ToolSettingsErrorCode;
   207→  const normalizedCode: ToolSettingsErrorCode = (
   208→    code === 'auth_required'
   209→    || code === 'cancelled'
   210→    || code === 'network'
   211→    || code === 'scope_fetch_failed'
   212→    || code === 'invalid_scope_identifier'
   213→    || code === 'unknown'
   214→  ) ? code : 'unknown';
   215→  return new ToolSettingsError(normalizedCode, failure.message, {
   216→    recoverable: failure.recoverable,
   217→    cause: failure.cause ?? failure.error,
   218→  });
   219→}
   220→/** The wrapper format returned by fetchToolSettingsSupabase */
   221→export interface SettingsFetchResult<T = unknown> {
   222→  settings: T;
   223→  hasShotSettings: boolean;
   224→}
   225→/**
   226→ * Get authenticated user ID without acquiring navigator.locks.
   227→ *
   228→ * Resolution order (all synchronous / lock-free):
   229→ *   1. In-memory cache (seeded during runtime bootstrap auth sync)
   230→ *   2. localStorage session (same key Supabase uses; contains full user object)
   231→ *   3. null — user is genuinely signed out
   232→ *
   233→ * Previously this called getSession() / getUser() which both acquire a shared
   234→ * navigator.lock. During token refresh Supabase holds an EXCLUSIVE lock, so
   235→ * ALL shared-lock requests queue behind it — blocking for 600ms-16s. By reading
   236→ * the user ID from localStorage instead we avoid locks entirely. Token validity
   237→ * for actual data requests is handled by createSupabaseClient's cached token.
   238→ */
   239→function buildUserLookupResult(userId: string | null): UserLookupResult {
   240→  return Promise.resolve({
   241→    data: { user: userId ? { id: userId } : null },
   242→    error: null,
   243→  });
   244→}
   245→
   246→function readFreshCachedUserId(): string | null | undefined {
   247→  if (!hasCachedUserSnapshot || (Date.now() - cachedUserAt) >= USER_CACHE_MS) {
   248→    return undefined;
   249→  }
   250→  return cachedUser?.id ?? null;
   251→}
   252→
   253→export function readCachedUserId(): UserLookupResult {
   254→  const cachedUserId = readFreshCachedUserId();
   255→  return buildUserLookupResult(cachedUserId ?? null);
   256→}
   257→
   258→export function resolveAndCacheUserId(): UserLookupResult {
   259→  const cachedUserId = readFreshCachedUserId();
   260→  if (cachedUserId !== undefined) {
   261→    return buildUserLookupResult(cachedUserId);
   262→  }
   263→
   264→  const localUserId = readUserIdFromStorage();
   265→  if (localUserId) {
   266→    updateCachedUserId(localUserId, false);
   267→    return buildUserLookupResult(localUserId);
   268→  }
   269→
   270→  updateCachedUserId(null, false);
   271→  return buildUserLookupResult(null);
   272→}
   273→type SettingsRow = { data: { settings: unknown } | null; error: unknown };
   274→function throwIfAborted(signal?: AbortSignal): void {
   275→  if (!signal?.aborted) {
   276→    return;
   277→  }
   278→  throw new ToolSettingsError('cancelled', 'Request was cancelled', {
   279→    recoverable: true,
   280→  });
   281→}
   282→type AbortableQuery<T> = {
   283→  abortSignal?: (signal: AbortSignal) => T;
   284→};
   285→function maybeAttachAbortSignal<T>(query: T, signal?: AbortSignal): T {
   286→  if (!signal) {
   287→    return query;
   288→  }
   289→  const abortable = query as AbortableQuery<T>;
   290→  if (typeof abortable.abortSignal === 'function') {
   291→    return abortable.abortSignal(signal);
   292→  }
   293→  return query;
   294→}
   295→async function raceWithAbort<T>(promise: Promise<T>, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<T> {
   296→  if (!signal) {
   297→    return promise;
   298→  }
   299→  throwIfAborted(signal);
   300→  return await new Promise<T>((resolve, reject) => {
   301→    const onAbort = () => {
   302→      cleanup();
   303→      reject(new ToolSettingsError('cancelled', 'Request was cancelled', {
   304→        recoverable: true,
   305→      }));
   306→    };
   307→    const cleanup = () => {
   308→      signal.removeEventListener('abort', onAbort);
   309→    };
   310→    signal.addEventListener('abort', onAbort, { once: true });
   311→    promise.then(
   312→      (value) => {
   313→        cleanup();
   314→        resolve(value);
   315→      },
   316→      (error) => {
   317→        cleanup();
   318→        reject(error);
   319→      },
   320→    );
   321→  });
   322→}
   323→/** Fetch settings from all three scopes (user, project, shot) in parallel. */
   324→function fetchAllScopes(
   325→  supabaseClient: ReturnType<typeof getSupabaseClient>,
   326→  userId: string,
   327→  ctx: ToolSettingsContext,
   328→  signal?: AbortSignal,
   329→): Promise<[SettingsRow, SettingsRow, SettingsRow]> {
   330→  const userQuery = maybeAttachAbortSignal(
   331→    supabaseClient
   332→      .from('users')
   333→      .select('settings')
   334→      .eq('id', userId)
   335→      .maybeSingle(),
   336→    signal,
   337→  );
   338→  const projectQuery = ctx.projectId
   339→    ? maybeAttachAbortSignal(
   340→        supabaseClient
   341→          .from('projects')
   342→          .select('settings')
   343→          .eq('id', ctx.projectId)
   344→          .maybeSingle(),
   345→        signal,
   346→      )
   347→    : Promise.resolve({ data: null, error: null });
   348→  const shotQuery = ctx.shotId
   349→    ? maybeAttachAbortSignal(
   350→        supabaseClient
   351→          .from('shots')
   352→          .select('settings')
   353→          .eq('id', ctx.shotId)
   354→          .maybeSingle(),
   355→        signal,
   356→      )
   357→    : Promise.resolve({ data: null, error: null });
   358→  return Promise.all([
   359→    userQuery,
   360→    projectQuery,
   361→    shotQuery,
   362→  ]) as Promise<[SettingsRow, SettingsRow, SettingsRow]>;
   363→}
   364→/** Extract tool-specific settings from scope results and merge in priority order. */
   365→function extractAndMergeSettings(
   366→  userResult: SettingsRow,
   367→  projectResult: SettingsRow,
   368→  shotResult: SettingsRow,
   369→  toolId: string,
   370→  ctx: ToolSettingsContext,
   371→): SettingsFetchResult {
   372→  if (userResult.error) {
   373→    throw new ToolSettingsError(
   374→      'scope_fetch_failed',
   375→      `Failed to load user settings: ${getErrorMessage(userResult.error)}`,
   376→      { recoverable: true, cause: userResult.error, metadata: { scope: 'user' } },
   377→    );
   378→  }
   379→  if (ctx.projectId && projectResult.error) {
   380→    throw new ToolSettingsError(
   381→      'scope_fetch_failed',
   382→      `Failed to load project settings: ${getErrorMessage(projectResult.error)}`,
   383→      { recoverable: true, cause: projectResult.error, metadata: { scope: 'project', projectId: ctx.projectId } },
   384→    );
   385→  }
   386→  if (ctx.shotId && shotResult.error) {
   387→    throw new ToolSettingsError(
   388→      'scope_fetch_failed',
   389→      `Failed to load shot settings: ${getErrorMessage(shotResult.error)}`,
   390→      { recoverable: true, cause: shotResult.error, metadata: { scope: 'shot', shotId: ctx.shotId } },
   391→    );
   392→  }
   393→  const userSettingsData = userResult.data?.settings as Record<string, unknown> | null;
   394→  const projectSettingsData = projectResult.data?.settings as Record<string, unknown> | null;
   395→  const shotSettingsData = shotResult.data?.settings as Record<string, unknown> | null;
   396→  const userSettings = (userSettingsData?.[toolId] as Record<string, unknown>) ?? {};
   397→  const projectSettings = (projectSettingsData?.[toolId] as Record<string, unknown>) ?? {};
   398→  const shotSettings = (shotSettingsData?.[toolId] as Record<string, unknown>) ?? {};
   399→  const defaultSettings = (toolDefaultsRegistry[toolId] as Record<string, unknown> | undefined) ?? {};
   400→  const hasShotSettings = shotSettings && Object.keys(shotSettings).length > 0;
   401→  const merged = deepMerge(
   402→    {},
   403→    defaultSettings,
   404→    userSettings,
   405→    projectSettings,
   406→    shotSettings
   407→  );
   408→  return { settings: merged, hasShotSettings };
   409→}
   410→/**
   411→ * Fetch and merge tool settings from all scopes using direct Supabase calls.
   412→ *
   413→ * Pipeline: dedup → auth → fetchAllScopes → extractAndMerge.
   414→ * Uses single-flight deduplication for concurrent identical requests.
   415→ *
   416→ * @returns `{ settings, hasShotSettings }` wrapper
   417→ */
   418→export async function fetchToolSettingsSupabase(
   419→  toolId: string,
   420→  ctx: ToolSettingsContext,
   421→  signal?: AbortSignal
   422→): Promise<OperationResult<SettingsFetchResult>> {
   423→  try {
   424→    reportUnknownSettingsId(toolId);
   425→    if (signal?.aborted) {
   426→      return toOperationFailure(new ToolSettingsError('cancelled', 'Request was cancelled', {
   427→        recoverable: true,
   428→      }));
   429→    }
   430→    const { data: { user } } = await resolveAndCacheUserId();
   431→    if (!user) {
   432→      return toOperationFailure(new ToolSettingsError('auth_required', 'Authentication required'));
   433→    }
   434→    const userId = user.id;
   435→    const singleFlightKey = JSON.stringify({
   436→      toolId,
   437→      projectId: ctx.projectId ?? null,
   438→      shotId: ctx.shotId ?? null,
   439→      userId,
   440→    });
   441→    const existingPromise = inflightSettingsFetches.get(singleFlightKey);
   442→    if (existingPromise) {
   443→      const value = await raceWithAbort(existingPromise as Promise<SettingsFetchResult>, signal);
   444→      return operationSuccess(value);
   445→    }
   446→    const promise = (async (): Promise<SettingsFetchResult> => {
   447→      throwIfAborted(signal);
   448→      throwIfAborted(signal);
   449→      const supabaseClient = getSupabaseClient();
   450→      const [userResult, projectResult, shotResult] = await fetchAllScopes(supabaseClient, userId, ctx, signal);
   451→      throwIfAborted(signal);
   452→      const { data: { user: latestUser } } = await resolveAndCacheUserId();
   453→      if (!latestUser || latestUser.id !== userId) {
   454→        throw new ToolSettingsError('cancelled', 'Request was cancelled due to auth state change', {
   455→          recoverable: true,
   456→          metadata: { expectedUserId: userId, latestUserId: latestUser?.id ?? null },
   457→        });
   458→      }
   459→      return extractAndMergeSettings(userResult, projectResult, shotResult, toolId, ctx);
   460→    })();
   461→    inflightSettingsFetches.set(singleFlightKey, promise);
   462→    promise.finally(() => {
   463→      inflightSettingsFetches.delete(singleFlightKey);
   464→    }).catch(() => {});
   465→    const value = await raceWithAbort(promise, signal);
   466→    return operationSuccess(value);
   467→  } catch (error: unknown) {
   468→    if (isToolSettingsError(error) && error.code === 'cancelled') {
   469→      return toOperationFailure(error);
   470→    }
   471→    if (isCancellationError(error)) {
   472→      return toOperationFailure(new ToolSettingsError('cancelled', 'Request was cancelled', {
   473→        recoverable: true,
   474→        cause: error,
   475→      }));
   476→    }
   477→    const errorMsg = getErrorMessage(error);
   478→    const contextInfo = {
   479→      visibilityState: typeof document !== 'undefined' ? document.visibilityState : 'unknown',
   480→      hidden: typeof document !== 'undefined' ? document.hidden : false,
   481→      online: typeof navigator !== 'undefined' ? navigator.onLine : true,
   482→    };
   483→    if (errorMsg.includes('Auth timeout') || errorMsg.includes('Auth request was cancelled')) {
   484→      normalizeAndPresentError(error, {
   485→        context: 'fetchToolSettingsSupabase.authTimeout',
   486→        showToast: false,
   487→        logData: contextInfo,
   488→      });
   489→      return toOperationFailure(new ToolSettingsError(
   490→        'network',
   491→        'Authentication check timed out. Please retry.',
   492→        {
   493→          recoverable: true,
   494→          cause: error,
   495→          metadata: { ...contextInfo, reason: 'auth_timeout' },
   496→        },
   497→      ));
   498→    }
   499→    if (errorMsg.includes('Failed to fetch')) {
   500→      normalizeAndPresentError(error, { context: 'fetchToolSettingsSupabase.network', showToast: false, logData: contextInfo });
   501→      return toOperationFailure(new ToolSettingsError(
   502→        'network',
   503→        'Network connection issue. Please check your internet connection.',
   504→        {
   505→          recoverable: true,
   506→          cause: error,
   507→          metadata: contextInfo,
   508→        },
   509→      ));
   510→    }
   511→    normalizeAndPresentError(error, { context: 'fetchToolSettingsSupabase', showToast: false, logData: contextInfo });
   512→    return toOperationFailure(classifyToolSettingsError(error));
   513→  }
   514→}
   515→/**
   516→ * Fetch tool settings and throw `ToolSettingsError` on failure.
   517→ *
   518→ * Use this from hook boundaries that already model errors as thrown exceptions
   519→ * (for example React Query query functions and mutation pipelines) so read/write
   520→ * settings flows share the same error contract.
   521→ */
   522→export async function fetchToolSettingsSupabaseOrThrow(
   523→  toolId: string,
   524→  ctx: ToolSettingsContext,
   525→  signal?: AbortSignal,
   526→): Promise<SettingsFetchResult> {
   527→  const result = await fetchToolSettingsSupabase(toolId, ctx, signal);
   528→  if (!result.ok) {
   529→    throw toToolSettingsErrorFromOperationFailure(result);
   530→  }
   531→  return result.value;
   532→}

1→/**
     2→ * PreloadingService - Unified coordinator for all image preloading
     3→ *
     4→ * This is the single source of truth for:
     5→ * 1. Preload queue management (network concurrency)
     6→ * 2. Lifecycle coordination (project switch, deletions, connection changes)
     7→ *
     8→ * The tracker (tracker.ts) is the source of truth for what's been loaded.
     9→ * This service coordinates preloading and lifecycle events.
    10→ */
    11→
    12→import { normalizeAndPresentError } from '@/shared/lib/errorHandling/runtimeError';
    13→import { PreloadQueue } from './queue';
    14→import { getPreloadConfig } from './config';
    15→import { preloadImages } from './preloader';
    16→import {
    17→  clearAllLoadedImages,
    18→  clearLoadedImages,
    19→  getLoadTrackerStats,
    20→} from './tracker';
    21→import { PRIORITY } from './types';
    22→import type {
    23→  PreloadConfig,
    24→  PreloadableImage,
    25→  PreloadingServiceState,
    26→  PreloadingEvent,
    27→  PreloadingSubscriber,
    28→} from './types';
    29→import { dataFreshnessManager } from '@/shared/realtime/DataFreshnessManager';
    30→
    31→// =============================================================================
    32→// SERVICE
    33→// =============================================================================
    34→
    35→class PreloadingService {
    36→  private state: PreloadingServiceState = {
    37→    currentProjectId: null,
    38→    isConnected: true,
    39→    isPaused: false,
    40→  };
    41→
    42→  private queue: PreloadQueue;
    43→  private config: PreloadConfig;
    44→  private subscribers = new Set<PreloadingSubscriber>();
    45→  private connectionTrackingInitialized = false;
    46→
    47→  // Singleton
    48→  private static instance: PreloadingService | null = null;
    49→
    50→  static getInstance(): PreloadingService {
    51→    if (!PreloadingService.instance) {
    52→      PreloadingService.instance = new PreloadingService();
    53→    }
    54→    return PreloadingService.instance;
    55→  }
    56→
    57→  private constructor() {
    58→    this.config = getPreloadConfig();
    59→    this.queue = new PreloadQueue(this.config.maxConcurrent);
    60→  }
    61→
    62→  /**
    63→   * Subscribe to DataFreshnessManager to track connection status.
    64→   */
    65→  initializeConnectionTracking(): void {
    66→    if (this.connectionTrackingInitialized) {
    67→      return;
    68→    }
    69→    this.connectionTrackingInitialized = true;
    70→
    71→    // Get initial status
    72→    const initialDiagnostics = dataFreshnessManager.getDiagnostics();
    73→    this.state.isConnected = initialDiagnostics.realtimeStatus === 'connected';
    74→
    75→    // Subscribe to changes
    76→    dataFreshnessManager.subscribe(() => {
    77→      const diagnostics = dataFreshnessManager.getDiagnostics();
    78→      const isConnected = diagnostics.realtimeStatus === 'connected';
    79→
    80→      if (isConnected !== this.state.isConnected) {
    81→        this.onConnectionStatusChange(isConnected);
    82→      }
    83→    });
    84→  }
    85→
    86→  // ===========================================================================
    87→  // LIFECYCLE EVENTS
    88→  // ===========================================================================
    89→
    90→  /**
    91→   * Called when the selected project changes.
    92→   * Clears all preloading state for the old project.
    93→   */
    94→  onProjectChange(projectId: string | null): void {
    95→    if (projectId === this.state.currentProjectId) {
    96→      return;
    97→    }
    98→
    99→    const previousProject = this.state.currentProjectId;
   100→
   101→    // Clear queue
   102→    this.queue.clear();
   103→
   104→    // Clear load tracker (single source of truth for what's loaded)
   105→    const clearedCount = clearAllLoadedImages('project switch');
   106→
   107→    this.state.currentProjectId = projectId;
   108→
   109→    this.emit({
   110→      type: 'project-changed',
   111→      data: { from: previousProject, to: projectId, clearedCount },
   112→    });
   113→  }
   114→
   115→  /**
   116→   * Called when generations are deleted (via realtime or mutation).
   117→   * Removes them from the load tracker.
   118→   */
   119→  onGenerationsDeleted(generationIds: string[]): void {
   120→    if (generationIds.length === 0) return;
   121→
   122→    // Remove from load tracker (the source of truth)
   123→    const clearedCount = clearLoadedImages(
   124→      generationIds.map((id) => ({ id }))
   125→    );
   126→
   127→    this.emit({
   128→      type: 'generations-deleted',
   129→      data: { ids: generationIds, clearedCount },
   130→    });
   131→  }
   132→
   133→  /**
   134→   * Called when connection status changes (from DataFreshnessManager).
   135→   * Could be used to adjust preloading strategy.
   136→   */
   137→  onConnectionStatusChange(isConnected: boolean): void {
   138→    if (isConnected === this.state.isConnected) return;
   139→
   140→    this.state.isConnected = isConnected;
   141→
   142→    // If disconnected, pause aggressive preloading
   143→    if (!isConnected) {
   144→      this.queue.clear();
   145→    }
   146→
   147→    this.emit({
   148→      type: 'connection-changed',
   149→      data: { isConnected },
   150→    });
   151→  }
   152→
   153→  /**
   154→   * Pause all preloading (e.g., when lightbox is open).
   155→   */
   156→  pause(): void {
   157→    if (this.state.isPaused) return;
   158→    this.state.isPaused = true;
   159→    this.queue.clear();
   160→  }
   161→
   162→  /**
   163→   * Resume preloading.
   164→   */
   165→  resume(): void {
   166→    if (!this.state.isPaused) return;
   167→    this.state.isPaused = false;
   168→  }
   169→
   170→  // ===========================================================================
   171→  // PRELOADING API
   172→  // ===========================================================================
   173→
   174→  /**
   175→   * Preload a batch of images.
   176→   * Uses the shared queue to avoid overwhelming the network.
   177→   */
   178→  async preloadImages(
   179→    images: PreloadableImage[],
   180→    priority: number = PRIORITY.normal
   181→  ): Promise<void> {
   182→    if (this.state.isPaused || !this.state.isConnected) {
   183→      return;
   184→    }
   185→
   186→    await preloadImages(images, this.queue, this.config, priority);
   187→
   188→    this.emit({ type: 'queue-updated' });
   189→  }
   190→
   191→  /**
   192→   * Get the current config.
   193→   */
   194→  getConfig(): PreloadConfig {
   195→    return this.config;
   196→  }
   197→
   198→  // ===========================================================================
   199→  // SUBSCRIPTION
   200→  // ===========================================================================
   201→
   202→  subscribe(callback: PreloadingSubscriber): () => void {
   203→    this.subscribers.add(callback);
   204→    return () => this.subscribers.delete(callback);
   205→  }
   206→
   207→  private emit(event: PreloadingEvent): void {
   208→    this.subscribers.forEach((callback) => {
   209→      try {
   210→        callback(event);
   211→      } catch (err) {
   212→        normalizeAndPresentError(err, { context: 'PreloadingService.subscriber', showToast: false });
   213→      }
   214→    });
   215→  }
   216→
   217→  // ===========================================================================
   218→  // DEBUGGING
   219→  // ===========================================================================
   220→
   221→  getDiagnostics(): {
   222→    state: PreloadingServiceState;
   223→    queue: { size: number; active: number };
   224→    tracker: ReturnType<typeof getLoadTrackerStats>;
   225→  } {
   226→    return {
   227→      state: { ...this.state },
   228→      queue: {
   229→        size: this.queue.size,
   230→        active: this.queue.activeCount,
   231→      },
   232→      tracker: getLoadTrackerStats(),
   233→    };
   234→  }
   235→}
   236→
   237→// =============================================================================
   238→// SINGLETON EXPORT
   239→// =============================================================================
   240→
   241→export const preloadingService = PreloadingService.getInstance();
   242→
   243→export function initializePreloadingService(): PreloadingService {
   244→  preloadingService.initializeConnectionTracking();
   245→
   246→  if (import.meta.env.DEV && typeof window !== 'undefined') {
   247→    (window as unknown as { __PRELOADING_SERVICE__?: PreloadingService }).__PRELOADING_SERVICE__ =
   248→      preloadingService;
   249→  }
   250→
   251→  return preloadingService;
   252→}
   253→

> AGENT

Done. Here's the review summary:

**2 commits + local changes reviewed:**

**Commit `2c47f559` "harden edge eligibility flows"** — Good overall. Rate limit API cleanup (denied = failure instead of success-with-allowed-false), `enforceRateLimit` simplifies callers, variant ownership check in trim-video. **But** it introduced a regression: `can_claim_more` dropped the capacity check. **Fixed.**

**Commit `2429933` "make runtime bootstrap explicit"** — Good overall. Typed settings decoders in `useUserUIState`, explicit bootstrap for preloading service, auth cache made explicit in `toolSettingsService`. **But** it had a duplicate `throwIfAborted(signal)` call. **Fixed.**

**Local changes** (uncommitted) — Clean. Inlines a trivial `createRecoveredErrorBoundaryState()` factory and moves files to better subdirectories (`clipboard` → `browser/`, `fileConversion` → `media/`, `generationTaskIdParser` → `tasks/`). Tests exist at the new locations. No broken imports (tsc passes).

**Pushed:** `bf420a7` with both fixes.

> DEVELOPER

are they generally making the code better?

> AGENT

Honestly, mixed. Let me be direct about each:

**Commit `2c47f559` "harden edge eligibility flows"** — Yes, genuinely good. The rate limit API was semantically wrong (denied was modeled as `ok: true, allowed: false` which is confusing), the `enforceRateLimit` helper cuts ~15 lines of boilerplate from every consumer, and the variant ownership check in trim-video is a real security fix. The capacity check regression was a slip, not a pattern problem.

**Commit `2429933` "make runtime bootstrap explicit"** — More mixed. The `useUserUIState` decoders are a real improvement — the old code was probably doing unsafe casts from JSON. But the `toolSettingsService` changes feel like refactoring for refactoring's sake — it was already ~400 lines and the commit added more surface area (the `initializeToolSettingsAuthCache` ceremony, the `AuthCacheSyncSource` interface) without clearly fixing a bug or simplifying callers. The duplicate `throwIfAborted` suggests the refactoring wasn't done carefully.

**The local changes** — Inlining `createRecoveredErrorBoundaryState()` is clearly better (a factory for a 3-field object literal is silly). The file moves are organizational opinion — `clipboard.ts` → `browser/clipboard.ts` doesn't make code easier to find if you already knew where it was. Not harmful, but not a clear win either.

**Overall pattern across the 188 commits on this branch:** There's been a lot of bot-driven churn. Many of the "share X" and "refactor: share Y" commits from earlier in the branch are the kind of mechanical deduplication that can fragment code without improving readability. The recent commits are higher quality — actual security fixes, API improvements, real correctness work — but they're still occasionally sloppy (duplicate calls, dropped conditions). The bot is doing useful work but needs closer review before merging.

    10→
    11→The strict score is a weighted average across 24 dimensions. The highest-weighted dimensions have the most leverage. This plan is organized by **impact** (weight × gap-to-95).
    12→
    13→---

    23→  - `canvasEnvironment` (refs, dimensions, upscale — 7 fields)
    24→  - `inpaintingState` (all inpainting + annotation mode — 20 fields)
    25→  - `transformState` (reposition, transform handlers — 8 fields)
    26→  - `generationState` (task submission states — 8 fields)

    28→  - **Consumers to update:** EditImagesPage.tsx + lightbox edit mode wrappers (~3-5 sites). Effort: moderate. Risk: moderate.
    29→- [x] **imageGeneration.ts param builders** — Extract 3 pure functions above the object literal:
    30→  - `buildLorasParam(loras, inSceneLoraUrl?)` → `{ additional_loras? }`
    31→  - `buildReferenceParams(styleRefImage, mode, settings)` → reference config object
    32→  - `buildHiresOverride(hiresScale, hiresDenoise, hiresSteps, additionalLoras)` → merged LoRA map
    33→  - Then task creation becomes clean assembly: `...buildLorasParam(...), ...buildReferenceParams(...), ...buildHiresOverride(...)`. Effort: moderate. Risk: safe (pure functions).
    34→- [x] **imageGeneration.ts lora validation duplication** — Already deduplicated. Shared `validateLoras()` called by both validate functions.
    35→- [x] **generateVideoService param count** — `buildTravelRequestBody` takes 23 params. Group into 3 sub-objects:
    36→  - `MotionParams` (amountOfMotion, motionMode, useAdvancedMode, effectivePhaseConfig, selectedPhasePresetId)
    37→  - `GenerationParams` (generationMode, batchVideoPrompt, enhancePrompt, variantNameParam, textBeforePrompts, textAfterPrompts)
    38→  - `SeedParams` (seed, randomSeed, turboMode, debug)
    39→  - Single call site at lines 621-643. Effort: moderate. Risk: safe.
    40→- [x] **clientThumbnailGenerator** — Already uses async/await cleanly. `readFileAsDataUrl()`, `loadImage()`, and `toBlob()` are each properly Promise-wrapped. No callback nesting.
    41→- [x] **useLineageChain N+1 queries** — Loop does one Supabase query per ancestor. Two options:
    42→  - **Option A (recommended): SQL RPC** with recursive CTE — `get_lineage_chain(variant_id)`, single round-trip, handles deep lineages. Requires: `npx supabase db push --linked` + function deploy. Effort: significant.
    43→  - **Option B (lighter): Batch query** — collect all variant IDs client-side first, then single `IN` clause fetch. Avoids RPC deployment. Effort: moderate.
    44→  - **Decision: Start with Option B** (no infrastructure change). Upgrade to RPC if lineage depths become a real performance issue.
    45→- [x] **Time formatting** — Already consolidated. Both `useProcessingTimestamp` and `useUpdatingTimestamp` delegate to `shared/lib/timeFormatting.ts`.

Strategy: Use `flushSync` from react-dom or restructure to set both states in a single synchronous handler (React 18 auto-batches). Read the handler first to understand what order dependency exists. Effort: moderate. Risk: moderate (state timing is subtle).
    55→- [x] **useAIInteractionService cleanup** — Remove unused `apiKey` param, delete trivial wrapper if it's just forwarding, remove stale comments. Effort: trivial. Risk: safe.
    56→- [x] **useApiKeys upsert** — Replace manual check-then-insert-or-update with Supabase `.upsert()`. Single operation, cleaner, avoids race conditions. Effort: trivial. Risk: safe.

    20→### Critical Issues Found
    21→
    22→#### 1. Import graph builder misses indented imports (causes 36 false orphans)
    23→
    24→**Root cause**: `detectors/py/imports.py` line 29 uses `grep -nE "^(import |from )"` which only matches top-level imports. This codebase uses lazy/deferred imports inside functions everywhere (for startup speed + avoiding circular imports). 31% of import lines are missed.
    25→
    26→**Fix**: Change grep pattern to `^\s*(import |from )` — one character fix. Drops orphaned false positives from 36 → ~3.
    27→
    28→**Secondary issue**: `from . import X` resolves to `__init__.py` instead of `./X.py`. This misses the `lang/__init__.py` → `lang/typescript.py` / `lang/python.py` connections.
    29→
    30→#### 2. eval/exec false positive (string literal match)
    31→
    32→**Root cause**: `detectors/py/smells.py` line 48 contains `"label": "eval()/exec() usage"` — the label string itself contains `eval()` which matches the detector's own regex. The detector is detecting its own documentation.
    33→
    34→**Fix**: The smell scanner should skip matches inside string literals, or at minimum not scan within `SMELL_CHECKS` constant definitions.
    35→
    36→#### 3. Magic number detector over-triggers (~23 false positives)
    37→
    38→Most "magic numbers" flagged are:
    39→- `* 100` / `/ 100` — percentage conversion (universal constant)
    40→- `50`, `200` — documented threshold bounds for heuristics (explained in output messages)
    41→- `300`, `500` — detector thresholds (the raison d'être of the tool)
    42→
    43→Only ~2 genuine magic numbers found (e.g., `bar_len = 15` for progress bar width).
    44→
    45→**Fix**: Exclude `100` (percentage scale), or require 4+ digits, or add allowlist for common threshold patterns.
    46→
    47→#### 4. Large + structural double-counting
    48→
    49→All 7 "large" findings also appear in the 26 "structural" findings. The structural detector already includes LOC as a signal. Having a separate "large" phase produces pure redundancy.
    50→
    51→**Fix**: Either (a) remove the separate `_phase_large` and let structural handle it, or (b) suppress structural findings that only have "large" as their sole signal.
    52→
    53→#### 5. Broad except is intentional pattern (22 findings, all low-value)
    54→
    55→Every detector file has `except Exception: continue` in its file-iteration loop. For a tool scanning hundreds of files, silently skipping unreadable files is the right call. These are technically true but not actionable — you don't want the scanner to crash on one bad file.
    56→
    57→**Could improve to**: `except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError, ValueError):` for specificity.
    58→
    59→#### 6. Complexity threshold too low
    60→
    61→Score of 6 triggers findings (minimum threshold in `detect_complexity`). 12 files have complexity 6-12. For small/medium files, this is noise.
    62→
    63→**Fix**: Raise minimum complexity score from 5 to 15+, or require score > LOC/20.

    95→### A) Missing React hook-correctness detection parity with ESLint
    96→
    97→Title:
    98→`TypeScript React: add detector support for rules-of-hooks and exhaustive-deps parity`
    99→
   100→Body:
   101→
   102→```md
   103→## Problem
   104→Desloppify does not currently surface React hook correctness violations comparable to ESLint's `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` and `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`.
   105→
   106→## Repro
   107→In `reigh`:
   108→
   109→```bash
   110→npm run lint
   111→# includes many react-hooks/rules-of-hooks + exhaustive-deps findings
   112→
   113→desloppify detect react --path . --json
   114→# returns count=0, entries=[]
   115→```
   116→
   117→Concrete example file from same run:
   118→- `src/shared/components/MediaLightbox/VideoLightbox.tsx` reported many conditional-hook-order errors in ESLint.
   119→
   120→## Expected
   121→Either:
   122→1. A dedicated detector for hook-order/dependency correctness, or
   123→2. Optional ESLint ingestion mode that maps these to structured findings.
   124→
   125→## Why this matters
   126→These are correctness bugs, not just style/architecture preferences, and currently create a false gap between scanner score and real code safety.
   127→
   128→## Acceptance criteria
   129→- Running scan on a repo with known `rules-of-hooks` violations emits corresponding findings.
   130→- Findings include file + line + rule class.
   131→- Detector can be enabled/disabled via config for teams that do not use React.
   132→```
   133→
   134→### B) Smells detector/state persistence mismatch for `any_type`
   135→
   136→Title:

   138→
   139→Body:
   140→
   141→```md
   142→## Problem
   143→`desloppify detect smells` reports `any_type` in files that do not receive corresponding persisted `smells::...::any_type` findings after full `scan`.
   144→
   145→## Repro
   146→Project: `reigh`
   147→
   148→```bash
   149→desloppify scan --path .
   150→desloppify detect smells --path supabase/functions/update-task-status --json
   151→# shows any_type matches
   152→

   154→# no match

   159→- files with persisted `smells::...::any_type`: 12
   160→- missing: 26
   161→
   162→## Expected
   163→If `detect smells` reports `any_type` for file X under the same scan profile/scope, persisted state should contain `smells::<file>::any_type` (unless explicitly ignored/suppressed with traceable reason).
   164→
   165→## Acceptance criteria
   166→- Detector output and scan-persisted findings are consistent for identical scope.
   167→- If filtered, state records why (suppressed, excluded, ignored) with machine-readable reason.

   169→
   170→### C) Scan-scope clarity: make implicit path explicit in output/status
   171→
   172→Title:
   173→`UX: make effective scan scope explicit and warn on implicit non-root default`
   174→
   175→Body:
   176→
   177→```md
   178→## Problem
   179→The effective scan scope can be implicit (e.g. project default source root), which is easy to misread as whole-repo scanning.
   180→
   181→## Repro
   182→Running `desloppify scan` may use configured default scope (e.g. `src`) while `desloppify scan --path .` produces a materially different raw finding count.
   183→
   184→## Expected
   185→Scanner output and status should prominently show:
   186→- effective `scan_path`
   187→- whether scope is implicit default vs explicit CLI override
   188→- optional warning when scope is not repository root
   189→
   190→## Why this matters
   191→Scope ambiguity leads to false confidence ("all clear") when entire subtrees are out-of-scope.
   192→
   193→## Acceptance criteria
   194→- `scan` and `status` print effective scope in first summary block.
   195→- non-root implicit scope emits a warning with suggested `--path .`.
   196→- query/state metadata keeps this explicit for historical comparisons.
   197→```

   218→## Wontfix Categories (with justification)
   219→
   220→### "No direct tests" (271 findings) — WONTFIX
   221→All 271 files have **transitive test coverage** via imports from tested modules. Writing direct unit tests for every file would be massive effort (weeks of work) with marginal return — the code IS tested, just indirectly. Focus test-writing effort on pure logic/utility functions that have complex branching, not on hooks and components that are integration-tested through their consumers.
   222→
   223→### Flat directories (14 findings) — WONTFIX
   224→Moving files into subdirectories doesn't improve code quality. It breaks `git blame`, creates merge conflicts, and makes existing mental models stale. The files in `src/shared/components/` (40 files) and `src/shared/hooks/` are findable via search. Directory restructuring is high-risk, zero-behavior-change work.
   225→
   226→### Magic numbers (29 findings) — WONTFIX
   227→These are domain-specific constants (animation durations, retry counts, buffer sizes, API limits). Extracting `const RETRY_DELAY_MS = 3000` is often LESS clear than `setTimeout(retry, 3000)` in context. The "magic number" rule is for business logic constants that appear in multiple places — these are all single-use.
   228→
   229→### Hardcoded URLs (13 findings) — WONTFIX
   230→API endpoint URLs, documentation links, CDN paths. These are configuration, not code smells. Moving them to a constants file adds indirection without value — the URL is the value, naming it doesn't help.
   231→
   232→### console.error without throw/return (10 findings) — WONTFIX
   233→These ARE the error handling. They log the error and continue execution. This is correct for non-fatal errors in UI code (clipboard failures, analytics failures, etc.). The alternative — throwing — would crash the UI.
   234→
   235→### Dead functions (most of 45 findings) — WONTFIX
   236→Investigated sample: nearly all are **intentional**:
   237→- `logInvalidationEvent`: empty by design, callsites preserved for debugging
   238→- `addCorruptionEvent`: guarded by `__CORRUPTION_TRACE_ENABLED__` flag
   239→- `triggerBrowserDownload`: actually has a real body (desloppify misdetected)
   240→- `transformGeneration`: actually has a real body (desloppify misdetected)
   241→- `noop`: used as default callback (could make props optional, but low-value)
   242→
   243→### Async without await (most of 20 findings) — WONTFIX
   244→Most are functions that return `runTaskCreationPipeline(...)` — they're async because the return type is `Promise<T>`, and the body is `return someAsyncCall(...)`. TypeScript doesn't require `await` before `return` in an `async` function — the promise is forwarded. This is correct code.
   245→
   246→### Subjective review stale (1051 findings) — WONTFIX
   247→"No human has reviewed this file" is metadata tracking, not a code quality issue. Running a holistic review would mark these resolved but wouldn't change any code.
   248→
   249→---

   392→
   393→## Phase 5: Wontfix & Deferred (~0hrs)
   394→
   395→These get wontfixed with specific justification:
   396→
   397→| Issue | ID | Justification |
   398→|-------|-----|---------------|
   399→| Feature-scale shared modules | 081f0d04 | ShotImageManager/ImageGenerationForm/MediaGallery ARE genuinely shared across tools. Moving 200+ files gains nothing. |
   400→| Media-lightbox mega-domain | 84709769, 3d7e4601 | 232 files, multi-week split project. Organizing hooks into subdirs (Phase 4) is sufficient. |
   401→| Frontend integration tests | ebabd382 | Valid but out-of-scope. Test infrastructure decision, not code quality. |
   402→| LoRA format coexistence | e8d4da87 | Read-side migration. Adding code to force-migrate adds complexity. Formats converge organically. |
   403→| Hardcoded colors (full sweep) | f51e6bda | 109 files, 532 occurrences. Far too large for this effort. Do incrementally per-PR. |
   404→| useSharedLightboxState "triple layer" | 3e2f3be5 | Investigated: does REAL composition (12+ sub-hooks, derived state). Not a pass-through. |
   405→| useEditSettingsPersistence "wrappers" | 014e348d | Investigated: dual-persistence coordination, race-condition protection. Not pass-through. |
   406→| useInlineEditState "forwarding" | b1cb3561, 646ebbb9 | Investigated: orchestrates 9+ sub-hooks, derives ImageEditState. Real composition, not deletable. |
   407→| useShotEditorLayoutPayloadModel | fbaecf94, 98ba9cca | Investigated: explicit field-mapping checklist for wide context provider. Explicitness aids maintenance. |
   408→| LightboxLayout model | 2e3f5fe9 | Investigated: memoization prevents re-renders. 6 context reads + 6 booleans is reasonable. |
   409→| No handler.ts integration test | 8c6e141c | Edge function integration tests require Supabase runtime. Out of scope. |
   410→| No frontend integration tests | ebabd382 | Infrastructure decision, not a code fix. |
   411→| SegmentSlotModeData god interface | d431cbf9 | 30+ fields but represents a genuine cross-boundary contract. Splitting would scatter the API. |

---
name: desloppify
description: >
  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker. Use when the user asks
  about code quality, technical debt, dead code, large files, god classes,
  duplicate functions, code smells, naming issues, import cycles, or coupling
  problems. Also use when asked for a health score, what to fix next, or to
  create a cleanup plan. Supports 28 languages.
allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
