# Desloppify

## 1. Your Job

Maximise the **strict score** honestly. Your main cycle: **scan → plan → execute → rescan**. Follow the scan output's **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS** — don't substitute your own analysis.

**Don't be lazy.** Do large refactors and small detailed fixes with equal energy. If it takes touching 20 files, touch 20 files. If it's a one-line change, make it. No task is too big or too small — fix things properly, not minimally.

## 2. The Workflow

Three phases, repeated as a cycle.

### Phase 1: Scan and review — understand the codebase

```bash
desloppify scan --path .       # analyse the codebase
desloppify status              # check scores — are we at target?
```

The scan will tell you if subjective dimensions need review. Follow its instructions. To trigger a review manually:
```bash
desloppify review --prepare    # then follow your runner's review workflow
```

### Phase 2: Plan — decide what to work on

After reviews, triage stages and plan creation appear as queue items in `next`. Complete them in order:
```bash
desloppify next                                        # shows the next workflow step
desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "themes and root causes..."
desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "comparison against completed work..."
desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "summary of priorities..."
desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "execution plan..."
```

### Automated triage (subagent runners)

For Codex: `desloppify plan triage --run-stages --runner codex`
For Claude: `desloppify plan triage --run-stages --runner claude` — then follow orchestrator instructions per stage

Options: `--only-stages observe,reflect` (subset), `--dry-run` (prompts only), `--stage-timeout-seconds N` (per-stage).

Then shape the queue. **The plan shapes everything `next` gives you** — don't skip this step.

```bash
desloppify plan                          # see the full ordered queue
desloppify plan reorder <pat> top        # reorder — what unblocks the most?
desloppify plan cluster create <name>    # group related issues to batch-fix
desloppify plan focus <cluster>          # scope next to one cluster
desloppify plan skip <pat>              # defer — hide from next
```

More plan commands:
```bash
desloppify plan reorder <cluster> top    # move all cluster members at once
desloppify plan reorder <a> <b> top     # mix clusters + findings in one reorder
desloppify plan reorder <pat> before -t X  # position relative to another item/cluster
desloppify plan cluster reorder a,b top # reorder multiple clusters as one block
desloppify plan resolve <pat>           # mark complete
desloppify plan reopen <pat>             # reopen
```

### Phase 3: Execute — grind the queue to completion

Trust the plan and execute. Don't rescan mid-queue — finish the queue first.

**Branch first.** Create a dedicated branch for health work — never commit directly to main:
```bash
git checkout -b desloppify/code-health    # or desloppify/<focus-area>
```

**Set up commit tracking.** If you have a PR, link it for auto-updated descriptions:
```bash
desloppify config set commit_pr 42        # PR number for auto-updates
```

**The loop:**
```
1. desloppify next              ← what to fix next
2. Fix the issue in code
3. Resolve it (next shows you the exact command including required attestation)
4. When you have a logical batch, commit:
   git add <files> && git commit -m "desloppify: fix 3 deferred_import findings"
5. Record the commit:
   desloppify plan commit-log record      # moves findings uncommitted → committed, updates PR
6. Push periodically:
   git push -u origin desloppify/code-health
7. Repeat until the queue is empty
```

Score may temporarily drop after fixes — cascade effects are normal, keep going.
If `next` suggests an auto-fixer, run `desloppify autofix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply.

**When the queue is clear, go back to Phase 1.** New issues will surface, cascades will have resolved, priorities will have shifted. This is the cycle.

### Other useful commands

```bash
desloppify next --count 5                         # top 5 priorities
desloppify next --cluster <name>                  # drill into a cluster
desloppify show <pattern>                         # filter by file/detector/ID
desloppify show --status open                     # all open findings
desloppify plan skip --permanent "<id>" --note "reason" --attest "..." # accept debt
desloppify exclude <path>                         # exclude a directory from scanning
desloppify config show                            # show all config including excludes
desloppify scan --path . --reset-subjective       # reset subjective baseline to 0
```

## 3. Reference

### How scoring works

Overall score = **40% mechanical** + **60% subjective**.

- **Mechanical (40%)**: auto-detected issues — duplication, dead code, smells, unused imports, security. Fixed by changing code and rescanning.
- **Subjective (60%)**: design quality review — naming, error handling, abstractions, clarity. Starts at **0%** until reviewed. The scan will prompt you when a review is needed.
- **Strict score** is the north star: wontfix items count as open. The gap between overall and strict is your wontfix debt.
- **Score types**: overall (lenient), strict (wontfix counts), objective (mechanical only), verified (confirmed fixes only).

### Subjective reviews in detail

- **Local runner (Codex)**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` — automated end-to-end.
- **Local runner (Claude)**: `desloppify review --prepare` → launch parallel subagents → `desloppify review --import merged.json` — see skill doc overlay for details.
- **Cloud/external**: `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` → follow session template → `--external-submit`.
- **Manual path**: `desloppify review --prepare` → review per dimension → `desloppify review --import file.json`.
- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state entries for correlation.
- Target-matching scores trigger auto-reset to prevent gaming.
- Even moderate scores (60-80) dramatically improve overall health.
- Stale dimensions auto-surface in `next` — just follow the queue.

### Review output format

Return machine-readable JSON for review imports. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template:

```json
{
  "session": {
    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
  },
  "assessments": {
    "<dimension_from_query>": 0
  },
  "findings": [
    {
      "dimension": "<dimension_from_query>",
      "identifier": "short_id",
      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
    }
  ]
}
```

**Import rules:**
- `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly (including `related_files`, `evidence`, and `suggestion`). Use `"findings": []` when no defects found.
- Import is fail-closed: invalid findings abort unless `--allow-partial` is passed.
- Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal or cloud session imports. Legacy `--attested-external` remains supported.

**Import paths:**
- Robust session flow (recommended): `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` → use generated prompt/template → run printed `--external-submit` command.
- Durable scored import (legacy): `desloppify review --import findings.json --attested-external --attest "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."`
- Findings-only fallback: `desloppify review --import findings.json`

### Review integrity

1. Do not use prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring.
2. Score from evidence only; when mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty.
3. Assess every requested dimension; never drop one. If evidence is weak, score lower.

### Reviewer agent prompt

Runners that support agent definitions (Cursor, Copilot, Gemini) can create a dedicated reviewer agent. Use this system prompt:

```
You are a code quality reviewer. You will be given a codebase path, a set of
dimensions to score, and what each dimension means. Read the code, score each
dimension 0-100 from evidence only, and return JSON in the required format.
Do not anchor to target thresholds. When evidence is mixed, score lower and
explain uncertainty.
```

See your editor's overlay section below for the agent config format.

### Commit tracking & branch workflow

Work on a dedicated branch named `desloppify/<description>` (e.g., `desloppify/code-health`, `desloppify/fix-smells`). Never push health work directly to main.

```bash
desloppify config set commit_pr 42              # link to your PR
desloppify plan commit-log                      # see uncommitted + committed status
desloppify plan commit-log record               # record HEAD commit, update PR description
desloppify plan commit-log record --note "why"  # with rationale
desloppify plan commit-log record --only "smells::*"  # record specific findings only
desloppify plan commit-log history              # show commit records
desloppify plan commit-log pr                   # preview PR body markdown
desloppify config set commit_tracking_enabled false  # disable guidance
```

After resolving findings as `fixed`, the tool shows uncommitted work, committed history, and a suggested commit message. After committing externally, run `record` to move findings from uncommitted to committed and auto-update the linked PR description.

### Key concepts

- **Tiers**: T1 auto-fix → T2 quick manual → T3 judgment call → T4 major refactor.
- **Auto-clusters**: related findings are auto-grouped in `next`. Drill in with `next --cluster <name>`.
- **Zones**: production/script (scored), test/config/generated/vendor (not scored). Fix with `zone set`.
- **Wontfix cost**: widens the lenient↔strict gap. Challenge past decisions when the gap grows.
- Score can temporarily drop after fixes (cascade effects are normal).

## 4. Escalate Tool Issues Upstream

When desloppify itself appears wrong or inconsistent:

1. Capture a minimal repro (`command`, `path`, `expected`, `actual`).
2. Open a GitHub issue in `peteromallet/desloppify`.
3. If you can fix it safely, open a PR linked to that issue.
4. If unsure whether it is tool bug vs user workflow, issue first, PR second.

## Prerequisite

`command -v desloppify >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "desloppify: installed" || echo "NOT INSTALLED — run: pip install --upgrade git+https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify.git"`

<!-- desloppify-end -->

## Claude Code Overlay

Use Claude subagents for subjective scoring work. **Do not use `--runner codex`** — use Claude subagents exclusively.

### Review workflow

Run `desloppify review --prepare` first to generate review data, then use Claude subagents:

1. **Prepare**: `desloppify review --prepare` — writes `query.json` and `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json`.
2. **Launch subagents**: Split the review across N parallel Claude subagents (one message, multiple Task calls). Each agent reviews a subset of dimensions.
3. **Merge & import**: Merge agent outputs, then `desloppify review --import merged.json --manual-override --attest "Claude subagents ran blind reviews against review_packet_blind.json" --scan-after-import`.

#### How to split dimensions across subagents

- Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` for dimensions with definitions and seed files.
- Read `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` for the blind packet (no score targets, no anchoring data).
- Group dimensions into 3-4 batches by theme (e.g., architecture, code quality, testing, conventions).
- Launch one Task agent per batch with `subagent_type: "general-purpose"`. Each agent gets:
  - The codebase path and list of dimensions to score
  - The blind packet path to read
  - Instruction to score from code evidence only, not from targets
- Each agent writes output to a separate file. Merge assessments (average overlapping dimension scores) and concatenate findings.

### Subagent rules

1. Each agent must be context-isolated — do not pass conversation history or score targets.
2. Agents must consume `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` (not full `query.json`) to avoid score anchoring.

### Triage workflow

Orchestrate triage with per-stage subagents:
1. `desloppify plan triage --run-stages --runner claude` — prints orchestrator instructions
2. For each stage (observe → reflect → organize → enrich):
   - Get prompt: `desloppify plan triage --stage-prompt <stage>`
   - Launch a subagent with that prompt
   - Verify: `desloppify plan triage` (check dashboard)
   - Confirm: `desloppify plan triage --confirm <stage> --attestation "..."`
3. Complete: `desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "..." --attestation "..."`

<!-- desloppify-end -->

                       [--top TOP] [--output FILE] [--chronic] [--code]
                       [--notes FILE] [--no-budget]
                       [pattern]

positional arguments:
  pattern               File path, directory, detector name, issue ID, or glob

options:
  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  --state STATE         Path to state file
  --status {all,auto_resolved,false_positive,fixed,open,wontfix}
                        Filter by status (default: open)
  --top TOP             Max files to show (default: 20)
  --output FILE         Write JSON to file instead of terminal
  --chronic             Show issues that have been reopened 2+ times (chronic
                        reopeners)
  --code                Show inline code snippets for each issue
  --notes FILE          Path to investigation notes file to attach to a issue
  --no-budget           Bypass per-detector noise budget (show all matching
                        issues)

examples:

Based on my thorough investigation of the code, here are my verdicts:

## Desloppify Findings Verification

**1. supabase_repository_wrappers_without_boundary_value** — NOT WORTH FIXING
The wrappers do add value. They're thin, yes, but they provide a consistent interface (`ProjectScopedGenerationInput`, `GenerationScopedVariantInput`) that enforces scoping rules (projectId/generationId validation) at the function parameter level. This is useful defensive pattern. The repository abstraction itself is minimal, which is appropriate.

**2. data_url_conversion_forces_caller_specific_glue** — NOT WORTH FIXING
The null return is handled properly everywhere. All three callers (referenceDomainService, useBase64Migration, recropReferences) explicitly check `if (!processedFile)` and throw meaningful errors. The tests verify null behavior. This is a reasonable error boundary strategy.

**3. system_logger_object_dumping** — NOT WORTH FIXING
The logging is reasonable, not wasteful. The buffer pattern (lines 40, 61-69) batches writes efficiently. JSON.stringify is only called when context exists (line 73: `context ? JSON.stringify(context) : ''`), so there's no unconditional dumping. The implementation is sensible.

**4. discord_stats_comment_drift** — NOT WORTH FIXING
I reviewed the entire discord-daily-stats function. Comments are minimal and accurate. Lines 56-74 have a clear comment explaining task categorization. Line 136 explains the weekly aggregation logic. No misleading or drifted comments found.

**5. media_lightbox_hooks_flat_overload** — REAL PROBLEM
54 files in a single flat directory is objectively too much. This violates your own design principle of not allowing >10-15 hooks in a flat folder. These should be grouped into thematic subdirectories (e.g., `hooks/layout/`, `hooks/editing/`, `hooks/navigation/`, `hooks/state/`). This is a refactoring opportunity for better code organization.

**6. preset_primary_sample_uses_generation_term** — FALSE POSITIVE
No file or reference with this name exists. Grep found nothing. This appears to be a phantom finding or mislabeled issue. WONTFIX due to non-existent code.

**7. shot_editor_setup_comment_overload** — NOT WORTH FIXING
The comments are excellent. They're not overload—they're justified instrumentation. Each section (lines 79-81, 101-103, 111-113, 122-124, etc.) has clear purpose headers. The detailed comments (lines 82-98, 125-127, 145-148) explain *why*, not just *what*. This is model commenting for performance-critical hooks. Keep them.

## Summary
- **Real problems**: media_lightbox_hooks_flat_overload (needs subdirectory refactoring)
- **False positive**: preset_primary_sample_uses_generation_term (doesn't exist)
- **Not worth fixing**: all others (working as designed or functioning properly)

1→// deno-lint-ignore-file
     2→/**
     3→ * System Logger for Edge Functions
     4→ *
     5→ * Logs to both console (for immediate visibility) AND system_logs table (for persistence/querying).
     6→ * Uses a buffer pattern to batch database writes for efficiency.
     7→ *
     8→ * Usage:
     9→ *   const logger = new SystemLogger(supabaseAdmin, 'my-function-name');
    10→ *   logger.info('Processing request', { task_id, shot_id });
    11→ *   logger.error('Something failed', { task_id, error: err.message });
    12→ *   await logger.flush(); // Call before returning response
    13→ */
    14→
    15→import type { SupabaseClient } from './supabaseClient.ts';
    16→
    17→type LogLevel = 'DEBUG' | 'INFO' | 'WARNING' | 'ERROR' | 'CRITICAL';
    18→
    19→interface LogContext {
    20→  task_id?: string;
    21→  shot_id?: string;
    22→  user_id?: string;
    23→  [key: string]: unknown;
    24→}
    25→
    26→interface BufferedLog {
    27→  timestamp: string;
    28→  source_type: string;
    29→  source_id: string;
    30→  log_level: LogLevel;
    31→  message: string;
    32→  task_id: string | null;
    33→  metadata: Record<string, unknown>;
    34→}
    35→
    36→export class SystemLogger {
    37→  private supabaseAdmin: SupabaseClient;
    38→  private functionName: string;
    39→  private logPrefix: string;
    40→  private logBuffer: BufferedLog[] = [];
    41→  private defaultTaskId: string | null = null;
    42→
    43→  constructor(supabaseAdmin: SupabaseClient, functionName: string, defaultTaskId?: string) {
    44→    this.supabaseAdmin = supabaseAdmin;
    45→    this.functionName = functionName;
    46→    this.logPrefix = `[${functionName.toUpperCase()}]`;
    47→    this.defaultTaskId = defaultTaskId || null;
    48→  }
    49→
    50→  /**
    51→   * Set a default task_id that will be used for all subsequent logs
    52→   * Useful when you learn the task_id after construction
    53→   */
    54→  setDefaultTaskId(taskId: string | null) {
    55→    this.defaultTaskId = taskId;
    56→  }
    57→
    58→  private addToBuffer(level: LogLevel, message: string, context?: LogContext) {
    59→    const { task_id, ...metadata } = context || {};
    60→    
    61→    this.logBuffer.push({
    62→      timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
    63→      source_type: 'edge_function',
    64→      source_id: this.functionName,
    65→      log_level: level,
    66→      message,
    67→      task_id: task_id || this.defaultTaskId,
    68→      metadata
    69→    });
    70→  }
    71→
    72→  debug(message: string, context?: LogContext) {
    73→    console.log(`${this.logPrefix} ${message}`, context ? JSON.stringify(context) : '');
    74→    this.addToBuffer('DEBUG', message, context);
    75→  }
    76→
    77→  info(message: string, context?: LogContext) {
    78→    console.log(`${this.logPrefix} ${message}`, context ? JSON.stringify(context) : '');
    79→    this.addToBuffer('INFO', message, context);
    80→  }
    81→
    82→  warn(message: string, context?: LogContext) {
    83→    console.warn(`${this.logPrefix} ⚠️ ${message}`, context ? JSON.stringify(context) : '');
    84→    this.addToBuffer('WARNING', message, context);
    85→  }
    86→
    87→  error(message: string, context?: LogContext) {
    88→    console.error(`${this.logPrefix} ❌ ${message}`, context ? JSON.stringify(context) : '');
    89→    this.addToBuffer('ERROR', message, context);
    90→  }
    91→
    92→  critical(message: string, context?: LogContext) {
    93→    console.error(`${this.logPrefix} 🔥 CRITICAL: ${message}`, context ? JSON.stringify(context) : '');
    94→    this.addToBuffer('CRITICAL', message, context);
    95→  }
    96→
    97→  /**
    98→   * Flush all buffered logs to the database
    99→   * Call this before returning your response!
   100→   */
   101→  async flush(): Promise<{ inserted: number; errors: number }> {
   102→    if (this.logBuffer.length === 0) {
   103→      return { inserted: 0, errors: 0 };
   104→    }
   105→
   106→    try {
   107→      const { data, error } = await this.supabaseAdmin
   108→        .rpc('func_insert_logs_batch', { logs: this.logBuffer });
   109→
   110→      if (error) {
   111→        console.error(`${this.logPrefix} Failed to flush ${this.logBuffer.length} logs to database:`, error.message);
   112→        // Don't clear buffer on error - logs are lost but at least console has them
   113→        return { inserted: 0, errors: this.logBuffer.length };
   114→      }
   115→
   116→      const result = data || { inserted: this.logBuffer.length, errors: 0 };
   117→      const bufferSize = this.logBuffer.length;
   118→      this.logBuffer = []; // Clear buffer after successful flush
   119→      
   120→      console.log(`${this.logPrefix} Flushed ${bufferSize} logs to database`);
   121→      return { inserted: result.inserted || bufferSize, errors: result.errors || 0 };
   122→    } catch (e: unknown) {
   123→      console.error(`${this.logPrefix} Exception flushing logs:`, e?.message || e);
   124→      return { inserted: 0, errors: this.logBuffer.length };
   125→    }
   126→  }
   127→
   128→  /**
   129→   * Get the current buffer size (useful for debugging)
   130→   */
   131→  getBufferSize(): number {
   132→    return this.logBuffer.length;
   133→  }
   134→}
   135→

1→export const fileToDataURL = (file: File): Promise<string> =>
     2→  new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
     3→    const reader = new FileReader();
     4→    reader.onload = () => resolve(reader.result as string);
     5→    reader.onerror = (error) => reject(error);
     6→    reader.readAsDataURL(file);
     7→  });
     8→
     9→export const dataURLtoFile = (
    10→  dataUrl: string,
    11→  filename: string,
    12→  fileType?: string,
    13→): File | null => {
    14→  try {
    15→    const [metadata, payload] = dataUrl.split(',');
    16→    if (!metadata || !payload) {
    17→      throw new Error('Invalid Data URL format');
    18→    }
    19→
    20→    const mimeMatch = metadata.match(/:(.*?);/);
    21→    const mimeType =
    22→      fileType ||
    23→      (mimeMatch && mimeMatch[1]) ||
    24→      'application/octet-stream';
    25→    const decodedPayload = atob(payload);
    26→    const bytes = new Uint8Array(decodedPayload.length);
    27→
    28→    for (let i = 0; i < decodedPayload.length; i += 1) {
    29→      bytes[i] = decodedPayload.charCodeAt(i);
    30→    }
    31→
    32→    return new File([bytes], filename, { type: mimeType });
    33→  } catch {
    34→    return null;
    35→  }
    36→};
    37→
```

1→/**
     2→ * useShotEditorSetup - Initialization and setup logic for ShotEditor
     3→ *
     4→ * Extracts from ShotEditor:
     5→ * - Shot resolution (foundShot, lastValidShotRef, selectedShot)
     6→ * - Effective aspect ratio calculation
     7→ * - Image query and selector hooks
     8→ * - Stability refs for performance optimization
     9→ *
    10→ * @see Phase 3 of shot-settings-context-cleanup.md
    11→ */
    12→
    13→import React, { useMemo, useRef } from 'react';
    14→import { useProject } from '@/shared/contexts/ProjectContext';
    15→import { useShots } from '@/shared/contexts/ShotsContext';
    16→import { useShotImages } from '@/shared/hooks/shots/useShotImages';
    17→import { Shot, GenerationRow } from '@/domains/generation/types';
    18→import {
    19→  selectTimelineImages,
    20→  selectUnpositionedImages,
    21→  selectVideoOutputs,
    22→} from '@/shared/lib/shotImageSelectors';
    23→import { compareByCreatedAtDesc } from '@/shared/lib/sorting/createdAtSort';
    24→import type { Project } from '@/types/project';
    25→
    26→interface UseShotEditorSetupProps {
    27→  selectedShotId: string;
    28→  projectId: string;
    29→  optimisticShotData?: Shot;
    30→  batchVideoFrames: number;
    31→}
    32→
    33→interface UseShotEditorSetupReturn {
    34→  // Shot resolution
    35→  selectedShot: Shot | undefined;
    36→  foundShot: Shot | undefined;
    37→  shots: Shot[] | undefined;
    38→
    39→  // Project data
    40→  selectedProjectId: string;
    41→  projects: Project[];
    42→
    43→  // Aspect ratio
    44→  effectiveAspectRatio: string | undefined;
    45→
    46→  // Image data (from queries/selectors)
    47→  allShotImages: GenerationRow[];
    48→  timelineImages: GenerationRow[];
    49→  unpositionedImages: GenerationRow[];
    50→  videoOutputs: GenerationRow[];
    51→  contextImages: GenerationRow[];
    52→  isLoadingFullImages: boolean;
    53→
    54→  // Initial parent generations (for fast FinalVideoSection render)
    55→  initialParentGenerations: GenerationRow[];
    56→
    57→  // Stability refs for callbacks
    58→  refs: {
    59→    selectedShotRef: React.MutableRefObject<Shot | undefined>;
    60→    projectIdRef: React.MutableRefObject<string>;
    61→    allShotImagesRef: React.MutableRefObject<GenerationRow[]>;
    62→    batchVideoFramesRef: React.MutableRefObject<number>;
    63→  };
    64→}
    65→
    66→/**
    67→ * Hook that handles ShotEditor setup and initialization.
    68→ * Extracts shot resolution, image queries, and stability refs.
    69→ */
    70→export function useShotEditorSetup({
    71→  selectedShotId,
    72→  projectId,
    73→  optimisticShotData,
    74→  batchVideoFrames,
    75→}: UseShotEditorSetupProps): UseShotEditorSetupReturn {
    76→  const { selectedProjectId, projects } = useProject();
    77→  const { shots } = useShots();
    78→
    79→  // ============================================================================
    80→  // SHOT RESOLUTION
    81→  // ============================================================================
    82→  // [FlickerFix] Persist the last valid shot object to prevent UI flickering during refetches
    83→  // When duplicating items, the shots list might briefly refetch, causing selectedShot to be undefined
    84→  const foundShot = useMemo(
    85→    () => shots?.find(shot => shot.id === selectedShotId),
    86→    [shots, selectedShotId]
    87→  );
    88→  const lastValidShotRef = useRef<Shot | undefined>();
    89→
    90→  // Update ref if we found the shot
    91→  if (foundShot) {
    92→    lastValidShotRef.current = foundShot;
    93→  }
    94→
    95→  // Use found shot if available, otherwise fallback to:
    96→  // 1. Optimistic shot data (for newly created shots not in cache yet)
    97→  // 2. Cached version if shots list is loading/refreshing
    98→  // Only use cache fallback if shots is undefined/null (loading), not if it's an empty array (loaded but missing)
    99→  const selectedShot = foundShot || optimisticShotData || (shots === undefined ? lastValidShotRef.current : undefined);
   100→
   101→  // ============================================================================
   102→  // STABILITY REFS
   103→  // ============================================================================
   104→  // Refs for values used in callbacks that shouldn't trigger callback recreation.
   105→  // This prevents the cascade of callback recreations on every shot/settings change.
   106→  const selectedShotRef = useRef(selectedShot);
   107→  selectedShotRef.current = selectedShot;
   108→  const projectIdRef = useRef(projectId);
   109→  projectIdRef.current = projectId;
   110→
   111→  // ============================================================================
   112→  // ASPECT RATIO
   113→  // ============================================================================
   114→  // Compute effective aspect ratio: prioritize shot-level over project-level
   115→  // This ensures videos in VideoOutputsGallery, items in Timeline, and other components
   116→  // use the shot's aspect ratio when set, otherwise fall back to project aspect ratio
   117→  const effectiveAspectRatio = useMemo(() => {
   118→    const projectAspectRatio = projects.find(p => p.id === projectId)?.aspectRatio;
   119→    return selectedShot?.aspect_ratio || projectAspectRatio;
   120→  }, [selectedShot?.aspect_ratio, projects, projectId]);
   121→
   122→  // ============================================================================
   123→  // IMAGE QUERIES AND SELECTORS
   124→  // ============================================================================
   125→  // PERFORMANCE OPTIMIZATION: Use context images when available since they're already loaded
   126→  // Only fall back to detailed query if context data is insufficient
   127→  const contextImages = useMemo(
   128→    () => selectedShot?.images ?? [],
   129→    [selectedShot?.images]
   130→  );
   131→
   132→  // [ShotNavPerf] PERFORMANCE FIX: Always fetch full data in background, but don't block UI
   133→  // We'll use context images immediately while the query runs asynchronously
   134→  const shouldLoadDetailedData = useMemo(
   135→    () => !!selectedShotId, // Always load full data in editor mode for pair prompts, mutations, etc.
   136→    [selectedShotId]
   137→  );
   138→
   139→  // Always enable query to get full data (needed for mutations and pair prompts)
   140→  const [REDACTED] ? selectedShotId : null;
   141→
   142→  // CRITICAL: Only call useShotImages when we genuinely need detailed data
   143→  // Using disabled query when context data is available
   144→
   145→  // [ShotNavPerf] CRITICAL FIX: Pass disableRefetch during initial load to prevent query storm
   146→  // The query will still run once, but won't refetch on every render
   147→  const fullImagesQueryResult = useShotImages(queryKey, {
   148→    disableRefetch: false, // Let it fetch normally, we'll use context images as placeholder
   149→  });
   150→
   151→  const fullShotImages = useMemo(
   152→    () => fullImagesQueryResult.data ?? [],
   153→    [fullImagesQueryResult.data]
   154→  );
   155→  const isLoadingFullImages = fullImagesQueryResult.isLoading;
   156→
   157→  // All shot images - use query data when available, fall back to context images during transition
   158→  // This prevents the "flash to empty" when navigating between shots
   159→  // PERF: Memoize to prevent ShotImagesEditor re-renders when reference doesn't actually change
   160→  const allShotImages = useMemo(() => {
   161→    return fullShotImages.length > 0 ? fullShotImages : contextImages;
   162→  }, [fullShotImages, contextImages]);
   163→
   164→  // PERF: Derive filtered views from the single useShotImages query instead of
   165→  // calling selector hooks (useTimelineImages, useUnpositionedImages, useVideoOutputs).
   166→  // Each selector hook internally calls useAllShotGenerations(shotId), creating a separate
   167→  // React Query observer. 4 observers for the same query key amplify the render loop
   168→  // because each runs observer.setOptions() every render.
   169→  const timelineImages = useMemo(() => {
   170→    return selectTimelineImages(allShotImages);
   171→  }, [allShotImages]);
   172→
   173→  const unpositionedImages = useMemo(() => {
   174→    return selectUnpositionedImages(allShotImages);
   175→  }, [allShotImages]);
   176→
   177→  const videoOutputs = useMemo(() => {
   178→    return selectVideoOutputs(allShotImages);
   179→  }, [allShotImages]);
   180→
   181→  // PERF: Derive initial parent generations from fast videoOutputs cache
   182→  // This allows FinalVideoSection to show thumbnail immediately while full segment data loads
   183→  // Parent generations are videos with orchestrator_details (join output parents)
   184→  const initialParentGenerations = useMemo(() => {
   185→    return videoOutputs
   186→      .filter(v => {
   187→        const params = v.params as Record<string, unknown> | null;
   188→        return params?.orchestrator_details != null;
   189→      })
   190→      .sort(compareByCreatedAtDesc);
   191→  }, [videoOutputs]);
   192→
   193→  // Refs for stable access inside callbacks (avoid callback recreation on data changes)
   194→  const allShotImagesRef = useRef<GenerationRow[]>(allShotImages);
   195→  allShotImagesRef.current = allShotImages;
   196→  const batchVideoFramesRef = useRef(batchVideoFrames);
   197→  batchVideoFramesRef.current = batchVideoFrames;
   198→
   199→  return {
   200→    // Shot resolution
   201→    selectedShot,
   202→    foundShot,
   203→    shots,
   204→
   205→    // Project data
   206→    selectedProjectId: selectedProjectId ?? projectId,
   207→    projects,
   208→
   209→    // Aspect ratio
   210→    effectiveAspectRatio,
   211→
   212→    // Image data
   213→    allShotImages,
   214→    timelineImages,
   215→    unpositionedImages,
   216→    videoOutputs,
   217→    contextImages,
   218→    isLoadingFullImages,
   219→    initialParentGenerations,
   220→
   221→    // Stability refs
   222→    refs: {
   223→      selectedShotRef,
   224→      projectIdRef,
   225→      allShotImagesRef,
   226→      batchVideoFramesRef,
   227→    },
   228→  };
   229→}
   230→

1→// deno-lint-ignore-file
     2→import { serve } from "https://deno.land/std@0.224.0/http/server.ts";
     3→import { bootstrapEdgeHandler, NO_SESSION_RUNTIME_OPTIONS } from "../_shared/edgeHandler.ts";
     4→import { toErrorMessage } from "../_shared/errorMessage.ts";
     5→
     6→const LOG_PREFIX = "[DISCORD-DAILY-STATS]";
     7→
     8→// Chart color palette (dark-theme friendly)
     9→const COLORS = {
    10→  imagesGenerated: { bg: "rgba(129, 140, 248, 0.85)", border: "rgb(129, 140, 248)" },   // indigo-400
    11→  imagesEdited:    { bg: "rgba(251, 146, 191, 0.85)", border: "rgb(251, 146, 191)" },   // pink-300
    12→  videosGenerated: { bg: "rgba(110, 231, 183, 0.85)", border: "rgb(110, 231, 183)" },   // emerald-300
    13→};
    14→
    15→const CHART_THEME = {
    16→  legendTextColor: "#cbd5e1",
    17→  axisTextColor: "#64748b",
    18→  axisGridColor: "rgba(148, 163, 184, 0.08)",
    19→  backgroundColor: "#1a1a2e",
    20→} as const;
    21→
    22→interface DayBucket {
    23→  date: string;
    24→  images_generated: number;
    25→  images_edited: number;
    26→  videos_generated: number;
    27→}
    28→
    29→serve(async (req) => {
    30→  const bootstrap = await bootstrapEdgeHandler(req, {
    31→    functionName: "discord-daily-stats",
    32→    logPrefix: LOG_PREFIX,
    33→    method: "POST",
    34→    parseBody: "none",
    35→    auth: {
    36→      required: true,
    37→      requireServiceRole: true,
    38→    },
    39→    ...NO_SESSION_RUNTIME_OPTIONS,
    40→  });
    41→  if (!bootstrap.ok) {
    42→    return bootstrap.response;
    43→  }
    44→
    45→  const discordWebhookUrl = Deno.env.get("DISCORD_STATS_WEBHOOK_URL");
    46→
    47→  if (!discordWebhookUrl) {
    48→    return new Response("DISCORD_STATS_WEBHOOK_URL not configured", { status: 500 });
    49→  }
    50→
    51→  const { supabaseAdmin, logger } = bootstrap.value;
    52→
    53→  try {
    54→    logger.info("Starting daily stats collection");
    55→
    56→    // Query completed tasks grouped by day, categorized into 3 buckets
    57→    const { data: dailyStats, error: statsError } = await supabaseAdmin.rpc(
    58→      "func_daily_task_stats"
    59→    );
    60→
    61→    // Task type name → bucket classification (derived from task_types table)
    62→    const IMAGE_GENERATED_TYPES = new Set([
    63→      "wan_2_2_t2i", "single_image", "qwen_image_style", "qwen_image",
    64→      "qwen_image_2512", "z_image_turbo",
    65→    ]);
    66→    const IMAGE_EDITED_TYPES = new Set([
    67→      "image_inpaint", "image_upscale", "image-upscale", "annotated_image_edit",
    68→      "edit_travel_flux", "magic_edit", "image_edit", "qwen_image_edit", "z_image_turbo_i2i",
    69→    ]);
    70→    const VIDEO_GENERATED_TYPES = new Set([
    71→      "animate_character", "individual_travel_segment", "video_enhance",
    72→      "join_clips_orchestrator", "travel_orchestrator", "edit_video_orchestrator",
    73→      "join_final_stitch",
    74→    ]);
    75→
    76→    let buckets: DayBucket[];
    77→
    78→    if (statsError || !dailyStats) {
    79→      logger.warn("RPC func_daily_task_stats not available, using direct query", {
    80→        error: statsError?.message,
    81→      });
    82→
    83→      // Paginate to fetch all completed tasks (default limit is 1000)
    84→      const PAGE_SIZE = 1000;
    85→      const allTasks: { created_at: string; task_type: string }[] = [];
    86→      let offset = 0;
    87→      while (true) {
    88→        const { data: page, error: rawError } = await supabaseAdmin
    89→          .from("tasks")
    90→          .select("created_at, task_type")
    91→          .eq("status", "Complete")
    92→          .gte("created_at", "2026-02-08T00:00:00Z")
    93→          .order("created_at", { ascending: true })
    94→          .range(offset, offset + PAGE_SIZE - 1);
    95→
    96→        if (rawError) throw rawError;
    97→        if (!page || page.length === 0) break;
    98→        allTasks.push(...page);
    99→        if (page.length < PAGE_SIZE) break;
   100→        offset += PAGE_SIZE;
   101→      }
   102→
   103→      // Aggregate in memory
   104→      const dayMap = new Map<string, DayBucket>();
   105→      for (const task of allTasks) {
   106→        const date = task.created_at.substring(0, 10); // YYYY-MM-DD
   107→        if (!dayMap.has(date)) {
   108→          dayMap.set(date, { date, images_generated: 0, images_edited: 0, videos_generated: 0 });
   109→        }
   110→        const bucket = dayMap.get(date)!;
   111→        const name = task.task_type;
   112→
   113→        if (IMAGE_GENERATED_TYPES.has(name)) {
   114→          bucket.images_generated++;
   115→        } else if (IMAGE_EDITED_TYPES.has(name)) {
   116→          bucket.images_edited++;
   117→        } else if (VIDEO_GENERATED_TYPES.has(name)) {
   118→          bucket.videos_generated++;
   119→        }
   120→      }
   121→
   122→      buckets = Array.from(dayMap.values()).sort((a, b) => a.date.localeCompare(b.date));
   123→    } else {
   124→      buckets = dailyStats;
   125→    }
   126→
   127→    if (buckets.length === 0) {
   128→      logger.info("No stats data found");
   129→      await logger.flush();
   130→      return new Response(JSON.stringify({ message: "No data" }), {
   131→        status: 200,
   132→        headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
   133→      });
   134→    }
   135→
   136→    // Determine if we should aggregate by week (>90 days of data)
   137→    const shouldAggregateWeekly = buckets.length > 90;
   138→    const periodData = shouldAggregateWeekly ? aggregateByWeek(buckets) : buckets;
   139→
   140→    // Convert to cumulative
   141→    const chartData: DayBucket[] = [];
   142→    let cumImg = 0, cumEdit = 0, cumVid = 0;
   143→    for (const b of periodData) {
   144→      cumImg += b.images_generated;
   145→      cumEdit += b.images_edited;
   146→      cumVid += b.videos_generated;
   147→      chartData.push({ date: b.date, images_generated: cumImg, images_edited: cumEdit, videos_generated: cumVid });
   148→    }
   149→
   150→    // Calculate yesterday's stats
   151→    const yesterday = new Date();
   152→    yesterday.setUTCDate(yesterday.getUTCDate() - 1);
   153→    const yesterdayStr = yesterday.toISOString().substring(0, 10);
   154→    const yesterdayStats = buckets.find((b) => b.date === yesterdayStr) || {
   155→      date: yesterdayStr,
   156→      images_generated: 0,
   157→      images_edited: 0,
   158→      videos_generated: 0,
   159→    };
   160→
   161→    // Calculate all-time totals
   162→    const totals = buckets.reduce(
   163→      (acc, b) => ({
   164→        images_generated: acc.images_generated + b.images_generated,
   165→        images_edited: acc.images_edited + b.images_edited,
   166→        videos_generated: acc.videos_generated + b.videos_generated,
   167→      }),
   168→      { images_generated: 0, images_edited: 0, videos_generated: 0 }
   169→    );
   170→
   171→    // Generate chart via QuickChart.io
   172→    const chartUrl = await generateChart(chartData, shouldAggregateWeekly);
   173→
   174→    // Format date for title
   175→    const titleDate = yesterday.toLocaleDateString("en-US", {
   176→      month: "short",
   177→      day: "numeric",
   178→      year: "numeric",
   179→      timeZone: "UTC",
   180→    });
   181→
   182→    // Post to Discord
   183→    const embed = {
   184→      title: `Daily Stats — ${titleDate}`,
   185→      color: 0x6366f1, // indigo
   186→      fields: [
   187→        {
   188→          name: "Images Generated",
   189→          value: `**${yesterdayStats.images_generated.toLocaleString()}**`,
   190→          inline: true,
   191→        },
   192→        {
   193→          name: "Images Edited",
   194→          value: `**${yesterdayStats.images_edited.toLocaleString()}**`,
   195→          inline: true,
   196→        },
   197→        {
   198→          name: "Videos Generated",
   199→          value: `**${yesterdayStats.videos_generated.toLocaleString()}**`,
   200→          inline: true,
   201→        },
   202→      ],
   203→      image: chartUrl ? { url: chartUrl } : undefined,
   204→      footer: {
   205→        text: `All time: ${totals.images_generated.toLocaleString()} imgs generated · ${totals.images_edited.toLocaleString()} imgs edited · ${totals.videos_generated.toLocaleString()} videos`,
   206→      },
   207→      timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
   208→    };
   209→
   210→    const discordPayload = { embeds: [embed] };
   211→
   212→    const discordRes = await fetch(discordWebhookUrl, {
   213→      method: "POST",
   214→      headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
   215→      body: JSON.stringify(discordPayload),
   216→    });
   217→
   218→    if (!discordRes.ok) {
   219→      const errText = await discordRes.text();
   220→      throw new Error(`Discord webhook failed (${discordRes.status}): ${errText}`);
   221→    }
   222→
   223→    logger.info("Discord message sent successfully", {
   224→      yesterday_images_gen: yesterdayStats.images_generated,
   225→      yesterday_images_edit: yesterdayStats.images_edited,
   226→      yesterday_videos: yesterdayStats.videos_generated,
   227→      chart_url: chartUrl,
   228→    });
   229→    await logger.flush();
   230→
   231→    return new Response(
   232→      JSON.stringify({
   233→        success: true,
   234→        yesterday: yesterdayStats,
   235→        totals,
   236→        chart_url: chartUrl,
   237→      }),
   238→      { status: 200, headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" } }
   239→    );
   240→  } catch (error: unknown) {
   241→    const message = toErrorMessage(error);
   242→    logger.error("Failed to generate/send daily stats", { error: message });
   243→    await logger.flush();
   244→    return new Response(`Error: ${message}`, { status: 500 });
   245→  }
   246→});
   247→
   248→/**
   249→ * Aggregate daily buckets into weekly buckets (Mon-Sun weeks)
   250→ */
   251→function aggregateByWeek(buckets: DayBucket[]): DayBucket[] {
   252→  const weekMap = new Map<string, DayBucket>();
   253→
   254→  for (const bucket of buckets) {
   255→    const date = new Date(bucket.date + "T00:00:00Z");
   256→    // Get Monday of this week
   257→    const day = date.getUTCDay();
   258→    const diff = day === 0 ? -6 : 1 - day; // Monday = 1
   259→    const monday = new Date(date);
   260→    monday.setUTCDate(date.getUTCDate() + diff);
   261→    const [REDACTED]().substring(0, 10);
   262→
   263→    if (!weekMap.has(weekKey)) {
   264→      weekMap.set(weekKey, { date: weekKey, images_generated: 0, images_edited: 0, videos_generated: 0 });
   265→    }
   266→    const week = weekMap.get(weekKey)!;
   267→    week.images_generated += bucket.images_generated;
   268→    week.images_edited += bucket.images_edited;
   269→    week.videos_generated += bucket.videos_generated;
   270→  }
   271→
   272→  return Array.from(weekMap.values()).sort((a, b) => a.date.localeCompare(b.date));
   273→}
   274→
   275→/**
   276→ * Generate a chart image URL via QuickChart.io
   277→ */
   278→async function generateChart(
   279→  data: DayBucket[],
   280→  _isWeekly: boolean
   281→): Promise<string | null> {
   282→  const labels = data.map((d) => {
   283→    const date = new Date(d.date + "T00:00:00Z");
   284→    return date.toLocaleDateString("en-US", {
   285→      month: "short",
   286→      day: "numeric",
   287→      timeZone: "UTC",
   288→    });
   289→  });
   290→
   291→  const chartConfig = {
   292→    type: "line",
   293→    data: {
   294→      labels,
   295→      datasets: [
   296→        {
   297→          label: "Images Generated",
   298→          data: data.map((d) => d.images_generated),
   299→          borderColor: COLORS.imagesGenerated.border,
   300→          backgroundColor: COLORS.imagesGenerated.bg.replace("0.85", "0.15"),
   301→          borderWidth: 2,
   302→          fill: true,
   303→          tension: 0.3,
   304→          pointRadius: 0,
   305→        },
   306→        {
   307→          label: "Images Edited",
   308→          data: data.map((d) => d.images_edited),
   309→          borderColor: COLORS.imagesEdited.border,
   310→          backgroundColor: COLORS.imagesEdited.bg.replace("0.85", "0.15"),
   311→          borderWidth: 2,
   312→          fill: true,
   313→          tension: 0.3,
   314→          pointRadius: 0,
   315→        },
   316→        {
   317→          label: "Videos Generated",
   318→          data: data.map((d) => d.videos_generated),
   319→          borderColor: COLORS.videosGenerated.border,
   320→          backgroundColor: COLORS.videosGenerated.bg.replace("0.85", "0.15"),
   321→          borderWidth: 2,
   322→          fill: true,
   323→          tension: 0.3,
   324→          pointRadius: 0,
   325→        },
   326→      ],
   327→    },
   328→    options: {
   329→      plugins: {
   330→        title: {
   331→          display: false,
   332→        },
   333→        legend: {
   334→          position: "bottom",
   335→          labels: {
   336→            color: CHART_THEME.legendTextColor,
   337→            padding: 16,
   338→            usePointStyle: true,
   339→            pointStyle: "circle",
   340→            font: { size: 11 },
   341→          },
   342→        },
   343→      },
   344→      scales: {
   345→        x: {
   346→          ticks: { color: CHART_THEME.axisTextColor, maxRotation: 45, font: { size: 10 } },
   347→          grid: { display: false },
   348→        },
   349→        y: {
   350→          ticks: { color: CHART_THEME.axisTextColor, font: { size: 10 } },
   351→          grid: { color: CHART_THEME.axisGridColor },
   352→          beginAtZero: true,
   353→        },
   354→      },
   355→    },
   356→  };
   357→
   358→  try {
   359→    const res = await fetch("https://quickchart.io/chart/create", {
   360→      method: "POST",
   361→      headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
   362→      body: JSON.stringify({
   363→        backgroundColor: CHART_THEME.backgroundColor,
   364→        width: 800,
   365→        height: 350,
   366→        format: "png",
   367→        chart: chartConfig,
   368→      }),
   369→    });
   370→
   371→    if (!res.ok) {
   372→      console.error(`${LOG_PREFIX} QuickChart error: ${res.status}`);
   373→      return null;
   374→    }
   375→
   376→    const result = await res.json();
   377→    return result.url || null;
   378→  } catch (err) {
   379→    console.error(`${LOG_PREFIX} QuickChart request failed:`, err);
   380→    return null;
   381→  }
   382→}

    70→export async function uploadAndProcessReference(
    71→  input: UploadAndProcessReferenceInput,
    72→): Promise<OperationResult<UploadAndProcessReferenceResult>> {
    73→  try {
    74→    const dataURL = await fileToDataURL(input.file);
    75→    const originalUploadedUrl = await uploadImageToStorage(input.file);
    76→
    77→    let processedDataURL = dataURL;
    78→    if (input.selectedProjectId) {
    79→      const { aspectRatio } = await resolveProjectResolution(input.selectedProjectId);
    80→      const processed = await processStyleReferenceForAspectRatioString(dataURL, aspectRatio);
    81→      if (!processed) {
    82→        return operationFailure(new Error('Failed to process image for aspect ratio'), {
    83→          policy: 'fail_closed',
    84→          errorCode: 'reference_aspect_processing_failed',
    85→          message: 'Failed to process image for aspect ratio',
    86→          recoverable: false,
    87→          cause: { selectedProjectId: input.selectedProjectId },
    88→        });
    89→      }
    90→      processedDataURL = processed;
    91→    }
    92→
    93→    const processedFile = dataURLtoFile(
    94→      processedDataURL,
    95→      `style-reference-processed-${Date.now()}.png`,
    96→    );
    97→    if (!processedFile) {
    98→      return operationFailure(new Error('Failed to convert processed image to file'), {
    99→        policy: 'fail_closed',
   100→        errorCode: 'reference_file_conversion_failed',
   101→        message: 'Failed to convert processed image to file',
   102→        recoverable: false,

   105→
   106→    const processedUploadedUrl = await uploadImageToStorage(processedFile);
   107→    return operationSuccess({ originalUploadedUrl, processedUploadedUrl }, { policy: 'best_effort' });

   110→      policy: 'degrade',

   112→      message: 'Failed to upload and process reference image',
   113→      recoverable: true,
   114→      cause: error,

   146→          cause: {

   151→      }

   172→    name: `Reference ${(input.hydratedReferences.length + 1)}`,
   173→    styleReferenceImage: input.processedUploadedUrl,

   188→    createdAt: now,

   193→export function createReferencePointer(input: CreateReferencePointerInput): ReferenceImage {
   194→  const modeDefaults = input.referenceMode === 'custom'

   197→        subjectStrength: input.subjectStrength,
   198→        inThisScene: input.inThisScene,

   200→      }
   201→    : getReferenceModeDefaults(input.referenceMode, input.isLocalGenerationEnabled);

   206→    subjectDescription: '',
   207→    styleBoostTerms: '',
   208→    referenceMode: input.referenceMode,
   209→    createdAt: new Date().toISOString(),

    14→    const dataUrl = 'data:text/plain;base64,SGVsbG8=';
    15→    const file = dataURLtoFile(dataUrl, 'test.bin', 'application/octet-stream');
    16→    expect(file?.type).toBe('application/octet-stream');
    17→  });
    18→
    19→  it('returns null for invalid data URL', () => {
    20→    expect(dataURLtoFile('not-a-data-url', 'test.txt')).toBeNull();
    21→  });
    22→
    23→  it('falls back to application/octet-stream when MIME not in URL', () => {
    24→    const dataUrl = 'data:;base64,SGVsbG8=';
    25→    const file = dataURLtoFile(dataUrl, 'test.bin');
    26→    expect(file).toBeInstanceOf(File);
    27→    expect(file?.type).toBe('application/octet-stream');

1→import { processStyleReferenceForAspectRatioString } from './styleReferenceProcessor';
     2→import { uploadImageToStorage } from './imageUploader';
     3→import { dataURLtoFile } from './fileConversion';
     4→import { generateClientThumbnail, uploadImageWithThumbnail } from '@/shared/media/clientThumbnailGenerator';
     5→import { normalizeAndPresentError } from '@/shared/lib/errorHandling/runtimeError';
     6→
     7→/**
     8→ * Legacy reference image shape used by the recrop pipeline.
     9→ * Different from the modern ReferenceImage in @/shared/types/referenceImage
    10→ * which uses resourceId and optional fields.
    11→ */
    12→export interface RecropReferenceInput {
    13→  id: string;
    14→  name: string;
    15→  styleReferenceImage: string | null;
    16→  styleReferenceImageOriginal: string | null;
    17→  thumbnailUrl?: string | null;
    18→  styleReferenceStrength: number;
    19→  subjectStrength: number;
    20→  subjectDescription: string;
    21→  inThisScene: boolean;
    22→  referenceMode?: 'style' | 'subject' | 'style-character' | 'scene-imprecise' | 'custom';
    23→  styleBoostTerms?: string;
    24→  createdAt: string;
    25→  updatedAt: string;
    26→}
    27→
    28→/**
    29→ * Reprocesses all reference images for a project when dimensions change.
    30→ * Uses the original images as source and regenerates cropped versions.
    31→ * 
    32→ * @param references - Array of reference images to reprocess
    33→ * @param newAspectRatio - New aspect ratio string (e.g., "16:9", "1:1")
    34→ * @param onProgress - Optional callback for progress updates (current, total)
    35→ * @returns Promise with array of updated references
    36→ */
    37→export async function recropAllReferences(
    38→  references: RecropReferenceInput[],
    39→  newAspectRatio: string,
    40→  onProgress?: (current: number, total: number) => void
    41→): Promise<RecropReferenceInput[]> {
    42→  
    43→  const reprocessed: RecropReferenceInput[] = [];
    44→  
    45→  for (let i = 0; i < references.length; i++) {
    46→    const ref = references[i];
    47→    
    48→    // Skip if no original image
    49→    if (!ref.styleReferenceImageOriginal) {
    50→      reprocessed.push(ref);
    51→      onProgress?.(i + 1, references.length);
    52→      continue;
    53→    }
    54→    
    55→    try {
    56→      // Fetch the original image
    57→      const response = await fetch(ref.styleReferenceImageOriginal);
    58→      if (!response.ok) {
    59→        throw new Error(`Failed to fetch image: ${response.status} ${response.statusText}`);
    60→      }
    61→      const blob = await response.blob();
    62→      
    63→      // Convert to data URL for processing
    64→      const dataURL = await new Promise<string>((resolve, reject) => {
    65→        const reader = new FileReader();
    66→        reader.onloadend = () => resolve(reader.result as string);
    67→        reader.onerror = () => reject(new Error('Failed to read blob as data URL'));
    68→        reader.readAsDataURL(blob);
    69→      });
    70→      
    71→      // Reprocess for new aspect ratio
    72→      const processedDataURL = await processStyleReferenceForAspectRatioString(
    73→        dataURL,
    74→        newAspectRatio
    75→      );
    76→      
    77→      if (!processedDataURL) {
    78→        throw new Error('processStyleReferenceForAspectRatioString returned null');
    79→      }
    80→      
    81→      // Upload new processed version with thumbnail
    82→      const processedFile = dataURLtoFile(
    83→        processedDataURL,
    84→        `reference-${ref.id}-${Date.now()}.png`
    85→      );
    86→      
    87→      if (!processedFile) {
    88→        throw new Error('dataURLtoFile returned null');
    89→      }
    90→      
    91→      let newProcessedUrl = '';
    92→      let newThumbnailUrl = '';
    93→      
    94→      try {
    95→        // Generate thumbnail for reprocessed image
    96→        const thumbnailResult = await generateClientThumbnail(processedFile, 300, 0.8);
    97→
    98→        // Upload both main image and thumbnail
    99→        const uploadResult = await uploadImageWithThumbnail(processedFile, thumbnailResult.thumbnailBlob);
   100→        newProcessedUrl = uploadResult.imageUrl;
   101→        newThumbnailUrl = uploadResult.thumbnailUrl;
   102→        
   103→      } catch {
   104→        // Fallback to original upload flow without thumbnail
   105→        newProcessedUrl = await uploadImageToStorage(processedFile);
   106→        newThumbnailUrl = newProcessedUrl; // Use main image as fallback
   107→      }
   108→      
   109→      // Update reference with new processed URL and thumbnail (keep original)
   110→      const updatedRef = {
   111→        ...ref,
   112→        styleReferenceImage: newProcessedUrl,
   113→        thumbnailUrl: newThumbnailUrl,
   114→        updatedAt: new Date().toISOString()
   115→      };
   116→      reprocessed.push(updatedRef);
   117→      
   118→      // Report progress
   119→      onProgress?.(i + 1, references.length);
   120→      
   121→    } catch (error) {
   122→      normalizeAndPresentError(error, { context: 'RecropReferences', showToast: false });
   123→      // Keep the old reference unchanged on error
   124→      reprocessed.push(ref);
   125→      // Still report progress
   126→      onProgress?.(i + 1, references.length);
   127→    }
   128→  }
   129→  
   130→  return reprocessed;
   131→}
   132→
```

Based on my thorough investigation of the code, here are my verdicts:

## Desloppify Findings Verification

**1. supabase_repository_wrappers_without_boundary_value** — NOT WORTH FIXING
The wrappers do add value. They're thin, yes, but they provide a consistent interface (`ProjectScopedGenerationInput`, `GenerationScopedVariantInput`) that enforces scoping rules (projectId/generationId validation) at the function parameter level. This is useful defensive pattern. The repository abstraction itself is minimal, which is appropriate.

**2. data_url_conversion_forces_caller_specific_glue** — NOT WORTH FIXING
The null return is handled properly everywhere. All three callers (referenceDomainService, useBase64Migration, recropReferences) explicitly check `if (!processedFile)` and throw meaningful errors. The tests verify null behavior. This is a reasonable error boundary strategy.

**3. system_logger_object_dumping** — NOT WORTH FIXING
The logging is reasonable, not wasteful. The buffer pattern (lines 40, 61-69) batches writes efficiently. JSON.stringify is only called when context exists (line 73: `context ? JSON.stringify(context) : ''`), so there's no unconditional dumping. The implementation is sensible.

**4. discord_stats_comment_drift** — NOT WORTH FIXING
I reviewed the entire discord-daily-stats function. Comments are minimal and accurate. Lines 56-74 have a clear comment explaining task categorization. Line 136 explains the weekly aggregation logic. No misleading or drifted comments found.

**5. media_lightbox_hooks_flat_overload** — REAL PROBLEM
54 files in a single flat directory is objectively too much. This violates your own design principle of not allowing >10-15 hooks in a flat folder. These should be grouped into thematic subdirectories (e.g., `hooks/layout/`, `hooks/editing/`, `hooks/navigation/`, `hooks/state/`). This is a refactoring opportunity for better code organization.

**6. preset_primary_sample_uses_generation_term** — FALSE POSITIVE
No file or reference with this name exists. Grep found nothing. This appears to be a phantom finding or mislabeled issue. WONTFIX due to non-existent code.

**7. shot_editor_setup_comment_overload** — NOT WORTH FIXING
The comments are excellent. They're not overload—they're justified instrumentation. Each section (lines 79-81, 101-103, 111-113, 122-124, etc.) has clear purpose headers. The detailed comments (lines 82-98, 125-127, 145-148) explain *why*, not just *what*. This is model commenting for performance-critical hooks. Keep them.

## Summary
- **Real problems**: media_lightbox_hooks_flat_overload (needs subdirectory refactoring)
- **False positive**: preset_primary_sample_uses_generation_term (doesn't exist)
- **Not worth fixing**: all others (working as designed or functioning properly)

> DEVELOPER

is it codemonkey proof now?

> AGENT

Honestly, not fully. The guardrails I added are text in action_step detail fields — the bot will see them but there's nothing enforcing them. The bot that wrote those 188 commits already had CLAUDE.md telling it "don't over-abstract" and it still created passthrough wrappers, duplicate `throwIfAborted` calls, and dropped a capacity check.

The main risks that text guardrails won't prevent:

1. **The bot doesn't verify its own work.** It won't run `tsc` or tests after changes. The two bugs I caught today were silent — they compiled fine but were semantically wrong.

2. **Action plans reference specific line numbers** that will drift as code changes. By the time the bot reaches cluster #7, the line numbers from cluster #1's changes will be stale.

3. **Some items are genuinely hard judgment calls** — decomposing a 500-line hook requires understanding which pieces are coupled. The bot tends to create mechanical splits (lines 1-200 → file A, lines 201-400 → file B) instead of semantic ones.

What would actually make it codemonkey-proof:

- **Smaller batches with human review between commits** rather than letting it grind through all 27 steps
- **The `/loop` we set up** — having me check its work every 2 hours catches regressions early
- **Wontfixing more aggressively** — the composition-assembler-decomposition cluster (splitting 500-line hooks) is exactly the kind of work that produced the most regressions on this branch. You might want to skip or defer it entirely.

Want me to wontfix the higher-risk decomposition items, or is the current state good enough to let it run?

+function throwScopedMutationFailure(error: unknown, emptyMessage: string): never {
+  if (error instanceof Error) {
+    throw error;
+  }
+  if (
+    error &&
+    typeof error === 'object' &&
+    'message' in error &&
+    typeof (error as { message?: unknown }).message === 'string'

1→import { useMutation } from '@tanstack/react-query';
     2→import { normalizeAndPresentError } from '@/shared/lib/errorHandling/runtimeError';
     3→import {
     4→  resolveGenerationTaskMapping,
     5→  type GenerationTaskMapping,
     6→} from '@/shared/lib/generationTaskRepository';
     7→
     8→/**
     9→ * Canonical on-demand generation -> task mapping resolver.
    10→ * Preserves repository status semantics (`ok`, `not_loaded`, `missing_generation`, `scope_mismatch`,
    11→ * `invalid_tasks_shape`, `query_failed`) so callers can branch explicitly.
    12→ */
    13→export function useResolveGenerationTaskMapping() {
    14→  return useMutation<GenerationTaskMapping, Error, string>({
    15→    mutationFn: resolveGenerationTaskMapping,
    16→    onError: (error: Error) => {
    17→      normalizeAndPresentError(error, { context: 'GenerationTaskMapping', showToast: false });
    18→    },
    19→  });
    20→}
    21→

Everything looks clean this round. Here's the summary:

**Commit `6c8307ff` "untangle shared core boundaries"** — Good. File moves (`clipboard` → `browser/`, `fileConversion` → `media/`, `generationTaskIdParser` → `tasks/`) plus fixing barrel imports to direct imports (breaking import cycles). Also consolidated `useGenerationTaskMapping` to delegate to the repository instead of maintaining a parallel Supabase fetcher. All import updates match, tsc passes.

**Local uncommitted changes** — Also good. The bot is working on the `generation-repository-contracts` cluster from the queue:
- `useGenerationMutations.ts` went from ~350 → ~150 lines by moving scope validation into the repository where it belongs
- `generationMutationsRepository.ts` absorbed scope validation + result assertion (~100 → ~200 lines) — each function now owns its own validation
- `usePrimaryTaskMapping.ts` → `useResolveGenerationTaskMapping.ts` (clearer name, same behavior)
- Function renames: `getPrimaryTaskIdForGeneration` → `resolveGenerationTaskMapping` (more descriptive)

**No issues found this batch.** Nothing to fix or undo. The bot appears to be following the guardrails — consolidating instead of fragmenting, moving code to better locations instead of adding new abstraction layers.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Desloppify tool**: Codebase health scanner with scan → plan → execute cycle. Queue items are organized into clusters with action_steps.
   - **Branch**: `chore/desloppify-queue-2026-03-02` with 188+ commits from main, authored by "Arnold Bot"
   - **OperationResult pattern**: Used across the codebase for typed success/failure results
   - **Rate limit API refactoring**: Changed from `OperationResult<RateLimitResult>` (denied = ok:true, allowed:false) to `OperationResult<AllowedRateLimitResult>` (denied = ok:false)
   - **Generation repository contracts**: Consolidation of task mapping, scope validation, and mutations into proper boundaries
   - **Edge functions**: Supabase Deno edge functions with shared auth/rate-limit/logging infrastructure
   - **Travel structure migration**: Ongoing migration from legacy to canonical structure_video/structure_guidance shapes
   - **File organization**: Moving shared/lib root files into themed subdirectories (clipboard→browser/, fileConversion→media/, generationTaskIdParser→tasks/)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`supabase/functions/task-counts/index.ts`** (line ~501)
     - Fixed regression: `can_claim_more` lost capacity check
     - Change: `can_claim_more: queued_only > 0` → `can_claim_more: queued_only > 0 && active_only < 5`

     - Verified this migration already fixes worker_heartbeat_public_bypass finding

     - Moved clipboard.ts → browser/clipboard.ts, fileConversion.ts → media/fileConversion.ts, generationTaskIdParser.ts → tasks/generationTaskIdParser.ts

1→import { useState, useCallback, useRef, useEffect, useMemo } from 'react';

     3→import type { ActiveLora, LoraModel } from '@/domains/lora/types/lora';
     4→import type { LoraManagerOptions, LoraManagerState } from '@/domains/lora/types/loraManager';
     5→import { dedupeActiveLoras, shouldApplyLoraDefaults } from './loraStateHelpers';
     6→import { useLoraPersistence } from './loraPersistence';
     7→
     8→// Re-export types
     9→export type { ActiveLora } from '@/domains/lora/types/lora';
    10→export type { LoraModel } from '@/domains/lora/types/lora';
    11→export type { LoraManagerOptions, LoraManagerState } from '@/domains/lora/types/loraManager';
    12→export type UseLoraManagerOptions = LoraManagerOptions;
    13→export type UseLoraManagerReturn = LoraManagerState;
    14→
    15→const EMPTY_ACTIVE_LORAS: ActiveLora[] = [];
    16→
    17→export const useLoraManager = (
    18→  availableLoras: LoraModel[] = [],
    19→  options: LoraManagerOptions = {},
    20→): LoraManagerState => {
    21→  const {
    22→    projectId,
    23→    shotId,
    24→    selectedLoras: controlledSelectedLoras,
    25→    onSelectedLorasChange,
    26→    persistenceScope = 'none',
    27→    enableProjectPersistence = false,
    28→    persistenceKey = 'loras',
    29→    enableTriggerWords = false,
    30→    onPromptUpdate,
    31→    currentPrompt = '',
    32→    disableAutoLoad = false,
    33→  } = options;
    34→
    35→  const isControlledSelection = !!(controlledSelectedLoras && onSelectedLorasChange);
    36→  const [internalSelectedLoras, setInternalSelectedLoras] = useState<ActiveLora[]>([]);
    37→  const selectedLoras = useMemo(
    38→    () => (isControlledSelection
    39→      ? (controlledSelectedLoras ?? EMPTY_ACTIVE_LORAS)
    40→      : internalSelectedLoras),
    41→    [controlledSelectedLoras, internalSelectedLoras, isControlledSelection],
    42→  );
    43→
    44→  const [hasEverSetLoras, setHasEverSetLoras] = useState(false);
    45→  const [isLoraModalOpen, setIsLoraModalOpen] = useState(false);
    46→
    47→  const setSelectedLoras = useCallback((loras: ActiveLora[]) => {
    48→    if (isControlledSelection) {
    49→      onSelectedLorasChange?.(loras);
    50→      return;
    51→    }
    52→    setInternalSelectedLoras(loras);
    53→  }, [isControlledSelection, onSelectedLorasChange]);
    54→
    55→  useEffect(() => {
    56→    if (selectedLoras.length <= 1) {
    57→      return;
    58→    }
    59→    const deduped = dedupeActiveLoras(selectedLoras);
    60→    if (deduped.length !== selectedLoras.length) {
    61→      setSelectedLoras(deduped);
    62→    }
    63→  }, [selectedLoras, setSelectedLoras]);
    64→
    65→  const selectedLorasRef = useRef(selectedLoras);
    66→  useEffect(() => {
    67→    selectedLorasRef.current = selectedLoras;
    68→  }, [selectedLoras]);
    69→
    70→  const markAsUserSet = useCallback(() => {
    71→    setHasEverSetLoras(true);
    72→  }, []);
    73→
    74→  const latestPromptRef = useRef(currentPrompt);
    75→  useEffect(() => {
    76→    latestPromptRef.current = currentPrompt;
    77→  }, [currentPrompt]);
    78→
    79→  const handleAddLora = useCallback((loraToAdd: LoraModel, isManualAction = true, initialStrength?: number) => {
    80→    if (selectedLorasRef.current.find((selectedLora) => selectedLora.id === loraToAdd['Model ID'])) {
    81→      return;
    82→    }
    83→
    84→    if (!loraToAdd['Model Files'] || loraToAdd['Model Files'].length === 0) {
    85→      toast.error('Selected LoRA has no model file specified.');
    86→      return;
    87→    }
    88→
    89→    const loraName = loraToAdd.Name !== 'N/A' ? loraToAdd.Name : loraToAdd['Model ID'];
    90→    const hasHighNoise = !!loraToAdd.high_noise_url;
    91→    const hasLowNoise = !!loraToAdd.low_noise_url;
    92→    const isMultiStage = hasHighNoise || hasLowNoise;
    93→    const primaryPath = isMultiStage
    94→      ? (loraToAdd.high_noise_url || loraToAdd.low_noise_url)
    95→      : (loraToAdd['Model Files'][0].url || loraToAdd['Model Files'][0].path);
    96→
    97→    if (!primaryPath) {
    98→      toast.error('Selected LoRA has no valid model URL.');
    99→      return;
   100→    }
   101→
   102→    const newLora: ActiveLora = {
   103→      id: loraToAdd['Model ID'],
   104→      name: loraName,
   105→      path: (hasHighNoise ? loraToAdd.high_noise_url : primaryPath) ?? primaryPath,
   106→      strength: initialStrength || 1.0,
   107→      previewImageUrl: loraToAdd.Images && loraToAdd.Images.length > 0
   108→        ? loraToAdd.Images[0].url
   109→        : undefined,
   110→      trigger_word: loraToAdd.trigger_word,
   111→      lowNoisePath: hasLowNoise ? loraToAdd.low_noise_url : undefined,
   112→      isMultiStage,
   113→    };
   114→
   115→    setSelectedLoras([...selectedLorasRef.current, newLora]);
   116→    if (isManualAction) {
   117→      markAsUserSet();
   118→    }
   119→  }, [markAsUserSet, setSelectedLoras]);
   120→
   121→  const handleRemoveLora = useCallback((loraIdToRemove: string, isManualAction = true) => {
   122→    const loraToRemove = selectedLorasRef.current.find((lora) => lora.id === loraIdToRemove);
   123→    if (!loraToRemove) {
   124→      return;
   125→    }
   126→
   127→    setSelectedLoras(selectedLorasRef.current.filter((lora) => lora.id !== loraIdToRemove));
   128→    if (isManualAction) {
   129→      markAsUserSet();
   130→    }
   131→  }, [markAsUserSet, setSelectedLoras]);
   132→
   133→  const handleLoraStrengthChange = useCallback((loraId: string, newStrength: number) => {
   134→    setSelectedLoras(
   135→      selectedLorasRef.current.map((lora) => (
   136→        lora.id === loraId ? { ...lora, strength: newStrength } : lora
   137→      )),
   138→    );
   139→    markAsUserSet();
   140→  }, [markAsUserSet, setSelectedLoras]);
   141→
   142→  const handleAddTriggerWord = useCallback((triggerWord: string) => {
   143→    if (!enableTriggerWords || !onPromptUpdate) {
   144→      return;
   145→    }
   146→
   147→    const prompt = latestPromptRef.current || '';
   148→    const newPrompt = prompt.trim() ? `${prompt}, ${triggerWord}` : triggerWord;
   149→    onPromptUpdate(newPrompt);
   150→    latestPromptRef.current = newPrompt;
   151→  }, [enableTriggerWords, onPromptUpdate]);
   152→
   153→  const {
   154→    persistenceSettings,
   155→    isSavingLoras,
   156→    hasSavedLoras,
   157→    saveSuccess,
   158→    saveFlash,
   159→    handleSaveProjectLoras,
   160→    handleLoadProjectLoras,
   161→    renderHeaderActions,
   162→  } = useLoraPersistence({
   163→    projectId,
   164→    shotId,
   165→    persistenceScope,
   166→    persistenceKey,
   167→    disableAutoLoad,
   168→    enableProjectPersistence,
   169→    manager: {
   170→      selectedLoras,
   171→      selectedLorasRef,
   172→      availableLoras,
   173→      handleAddLora,
   174→      handleRemoveLora,
   175→      handleLoraStrengthChange,
   176→      markAsUserSet,
   177→      setHasEverSetLoras,
   178→    },
   179→  });
   180→
   181→  const shouldApplyDefaults = useMemo(() => shouldApplyLoraDefaults({
   182→    hasEverSetLoras,
   183→    selectedLoraCount: selectedLoras.length,
   184→    persistenceScope,
   185→    persistedLoras: persistenceSettings?.loras,
   186→  }), [hasEverSetLoras, selectedLoras.length, persistenceScope, persistenceSettings?.loras]);
   187→
   188→  return {
   189→    selectedLoras,
   190→    setSelectedLoras,
   191→    isLoraModalOpen,
   192→    setIsLoraModalOpen,
   193→    handleAddLora,
   194→    handleRemoveLora,
   195→    handleLoraStrengthChange,
   196→    hasEverSetLoras,
   197→    shouldApplyDefaults,
   198→    markAsUserSet,
   199→    ...(enableTriggerWords && { handleAddTriggerWord }),
   200→    ...(enableProjectPersistence && {
   201→      handleSaveProjectLoras,
   202→      handleLoadProjectLoras,
   203→      hasSavedLoras,
   204→      isSavingLoras,
   205→      saveSuccess,
   206→      saveFlash,
   207→      renderHeaderActions,

1→import { ACTIONS } from 'react-joyride';
     2→import { TOUR_STEPS } from './tourSteps';
     3→
     4→export const SHORT_DELAY_MS = 400;
     5→export const LONG_DELAY_MS = 1500;
     6→export const WAIT_FOR_TARGET_DELAY_MS = 800;
     7→export const WAIT_FOR_TARGET_RESUME_DELAY_MS = 100;
     8→
     9→export type TourEventName = 'openGenerationModal' | 'closeGenerationModal';
    10→
    11→export interface SpotlightAdvanceBehavior {
    12→  delayMs?: number;
    13→  lockTasksPane?: boolean;
    14→}
    15→
    16→export type JoyrideAdvanceBehavior =
    17→  | { type: 'advance' }
    18→  | {
    19→      type: 'pause';
    20→      delayMs: number;
    21→      dispatchEvent?: TourEventName;
    22→      lockGenerationsPane?: boolean;
    23→      lockTasksPane?: boolean;
    24→    }
    25→  | {
    26→      type: 'waitForTarget';
    27→      delayMs: number;
    28→      selector: string;
    29→      dispatchEvent?: TourEventName;
    30→      releaseGenerationsPane?: boolean;
    31→      resumeDelayMs: number;
    32→    }
    33→  | {
    34→      type: 'clickThenPause';
    35→      delayMs: number;
    36→      selector: string;
    37→    };
    38→
    39→const SPOTLIGHT_ADVANCE_BEHAVIORS: Partial<Record<number, SpotlightAdvanceBehavior>> = {
    40→  [TOUR_STEPS.OPEN_GALLERY]: { delayMs: SHORT_DELAY_MS },
    41→  [TOUR_STEPS.GENERATE_IMAGES]: { delayMs: SHORT_DELAY_MS },
    42→  [TOUR_STEPS.OPEN_FIRST_SHOT]: { delayMs: LONG_DELAY_MS },
    43→  [TOUR_STEPS.TASKS_PANE]: { delayMs: SHORT_DELAY_MS, lockTasksPane: true },
    44→};
    45→
    46→const JOYRIDE_ADVANCE_BEHAVIORS: Partial<Record<number, JoyrideAdvanceBehavior>> = {
    47→  [TOUR_STEPS.OPEN_GALLERY]: {
    48→    type: 'pause',
    49→    delayMs: SHORT_DELAY_MS,
    50→    lockGenerationsPane: true,
    51→  },
    52→  [TOUR_STEPS.GALLERY_SECTION]: { type: 'advance' },
    53→  [TOUR_STEPS.GENERATE_IMAGES]: {
    54→    type: 'pause',
    55→    delayMs: SHORT_DELAY_MS,
    56→    dispatchEvent: 'openGenerationModal',
    57→  },
    58→  [TOUR_STEPS.HOW_IT_WORKS]: {
    59→    type: 'waitForTarget',
    60→    delayMs: WAIT_FOR_TARGET_DELAY_MS,
    61→    selector: '[data-tour="first-shot"]',
    62→    dispatchEvent: 'closeGenerationModal',
    63→    releaseGenerationsPane: true,
    64→    resumeDelayMs: WAIT_FOR_TARGET_RESUME_DELAY_MS,
    65→  },
    66→  [TOUR_STEPS.OPEN_FIRST_SHOT]: {
    67→    type: 'clickThenPause',
    68→    delayMs: LONG_DELAY_MS,
    69→    selector: '[data-tour="first-shot"]',
    70→  },
    71→  [TOUR_STEPS.TASKS_PANE]: {
    72→    type: 'pause',
    73→    delayMs: SHORT_DELAY_MS,
    74→    lockTasksPane: true,
    75→  },
    76→};
    77→
    78→export function getSpotlightAdvanceBehavior(stepIndex: number): SpotlightAdvanceBehavior {
    79→  return SPOTLIGHT_ADVANCE_BEHAVIORS[stepIndex] ?? {};
    80→}
    81→
    82→export function getJoyrideAdvanceBehavior(
    83→  index: number,
    84→  action: string,
    85→): JoyrideAdvanceBehavior {
    86→  if (action === ACTIONS.PREV) {
    87→    return { type: 'advance' };
    88→  }
    89→
    90→  return JOYRIDE_ADVANCE_BEHAVIORS[index] ?? { type: 'advance' };
    91→}
